from other rows. On the other hand, this subtraction does not affect the determinant of
Z_{J\cup\{i\}}
, so the Laplace expansion by the column
j
expresses it as the same
(i,j)
minor multiplied by
(-1)^{j+|J\cap[i]|}Z_{i}^{j}
. In conclusion, both maximal determinants are given by the same product, hence
\langle Z_{J\cup\{i\}}\rangle=\langle(Z_{\neg i}^{\neg j})_{J}\rangle
.

The lemma follows by applying Lemma
4.11
back and forth. Note that all maximal determinants of
{\mathrm{inc}}_{i}\,C
vanish unless one of their
k+1
columns is
C^{i}
. Therefore, we restrict the summation to terms of the form
J\cup\{i\}
.

\displaystyle\langle\widetilde{Z}({\mathrm{inc}}_{i}(C))\penalty\ Z_{I}\rangle\;

\displaystyle=\;\sum_{J\in\binom{N\setminus I}{k}}s(J\cup\{i\},I)\;\langle({\mathrm{inc}}_{i}\,C)^{J\cup\{i\}}\rangle\;\langle Z_{I\cup J\cup\{i\}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\;\sum_{J\in\binom{N\setminus I}{k}}s(J,I)\;(-1)^{|I\cap[i]|}\;\langle C^{J}\rangle\;\langle(Z^{\neg j}_{\neg i})_{I\cup J}\rangle

\displaystyle=\;(-1)^{|I\cap[i]|}\;\langle{\widetilde{Z}_{\neg i}^{\neg j}}(C)\penalty\ (Z_{\neg i}^{\neg j})_{I}\rangle

The second line is obtained by the definitions of the sign
s(J,I)
and the map
{\mathrm{inc}}_{i}
, together with the identity of determinants shown above. Finally, note that this calculation holds in the case
k=0
as well, when there is a single term
J=\varnothing
, and the
0\times 0
determinant
\langle C^{J}\rangle=1
by convention.
∎

Lemma
4.21
implies the following useful result for functionaries.

Lemma 4.22
.

Let
C\in\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq}_{k,N}
and
i\not\in N
, and let
F(z_{I}:I\in\scalebox{0.8}{$\tbinom{N}{4}$})
be a homogeneous polynomial.
Let
F^{\prime}(z_{I})
be the polynomial obtained from
F
by the substitution
z_{I}\mapsto(-1)^{|I\cap[i]|}z_{I}
.
If
F(\langle\widetilde{Z}(C)\penalty\ Z_{I}\rangle)
has a fixed sign, then

\SIGN F^{\prime}(\langle\widetilde{Z}({\mathrm{inc}}_{i}C)\penalty\ Z_{I}\rangle)\;=\;\SIGN F(\langle\widetilde{Z}(C)\penalty\ Z_{I}\rangle)

Proof.

Let
s=\SIGN F(\langle\widetilde{Z}(C)\penalty\ Z_{I}\rangle)
.
For any positive matrix
Z\in\mathrm{Mat}^{>}_{(N\cup\{i\})\times[k+5]}
, let
j\in[k+5]
be some index such that
Z_{i}^{j}\neq 0
. As shown in the proof of the previous lemma,
\langle Z_{J\cup\{i\}}\rangle=\langle(Z_{\neg i}^{\neg j})_{J}\rangle
for all
J\in\tbinom{N}{k+4}
, hence
Z_{\neg i}^{\neg j}\in\mathrm{Mat}^{>}_{N\times[k+4]}
is a positive matrix as well. Thus
F((-1)^{|I\cap[i]|}\langle\widetilde{Z}({\mathrm{inc}}_{i}C)\penalty\ Z_{I}\rangle)=F(\langle\widetilde{Z}_{\neg i}^{\neg j}(C)\penalty\ (Z_{\neg i}^{\neg j})_{I}\rangle)
, and our assumption implies that the right hand side has the given fixed sign
s
.
Clearly
F((-1)^{|I\cap[i]|}\langle\widetilde{Z}({\mathrm{inc}}_{i}C)\penalty\ Z_{I}\rangle)=F^{\prime}(\langle\widetilde{Z}({\mathrm{inc}}_{i}C)\penalty\ Z_{I}\rangle)
, so
F^{\prime}
has the fixed sign
s
.
∎

Example 4.23
.

If
C\in\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq}_{k,1235678}
is such that
\langle\widetilde{Z}(C)\,1\,3\,5\,6\rangle\,\langle\widetilde{Z}(C)\,2\,5\,6\,7\rangle>0
for all positive
7\times(k+4)
matrices
Z
, then
\langle\widetilde{Z}({\mathrm{inc}}_{4}C)\,1\,3\,5\,6\rangle\,\langle\widetilde{Z}({\mathrm{inc}}_{4}C)\,2\,5\,6\,7\rangle<0
for all positive
8\times(k+5)
matrices
Z
.

We continue with the two matrix operations
x^{k}_{i}(t),\,y^{k}_{i}(t):\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq}_{k,N}\to\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq}_{k,N}
, which add to some column a
t
-multiple of an adjacent column, usually applied with
t\in(0,\infty)
. Recall from Definition
3.4
that these maps act as right multiplication
C\mapsto C\cdot[x_{i}(t)]
by
N\times N
matrices, and our notation
\overline{N}=\max N,\underline{N}=\min N
. These matrices are
[x_{i}(t)]=\mathrm{Id}_{N}+t\,\mathrm{E}_{i}^{i{{\pluscirc}1}}
and
[y_{i}(t)]=\mathrm{Id}_{N}+t\,\mathrm{E}_{i{{\pluscirc}1}}^{i}
, with the “overflow” exception that if
i=\overline{N}
and
k
is even then
t\,\mathrm{E}_{\max N}^{\min N}
or
t\,\mathrm{E}_{\min N}^{\max N}
is subtracted rather than added. The following lemma describes the effect of these transformations on the twistor coordinates.

Lemma 4.24
.

Let
C\in\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq}_{k,N}
,
Z\in\mathrm{Mat}^{>}_{N\times[k+4]}
,
I\in\tbinom{N}{4}
and
i\in N
.

(X)

Let
s\in[0,\infty)
let
Z^{\prime}=[x_{i}(s)]Z
and
C^{\prime}=x_{i}(s)\,C
. Then

\left\langle\widetilde{Z}^{\prime}(C)\penalty\ Z^{\prime}_{I}\right\rangle\;=\;\begin{cases}\left\langle\widetilde{Z}(C^{\prime})\penalty\ Z_{I}\right\rangle+s\,\left\langle\widetilde{Z}(C^{\prime})\penalty\ Z_{I\cup\{i{\pluscirc}1\}\setminus\{i\}}\right\rangle&\text{if $i\in I,i{\pluscirc}1\not\in I,i\neq\overline{N}$}\\
\left\langle\widetilde{Z}(C^{\prime})\penalty\ Z_{I}\right\rangle+(-1)^{(k-1)}\,s\,\left\langle\widetilde{Z}(C^{\prime})\penalty\ Z_{I\cup\{i{\pluscirc}1\}\setminus\{i\}}\right\rangle&\text{if $i\in I,i{\pluscirc}1\not\in I,i=\overline{N}$}\\
\left\langle\widetilde{Z}(C^{\prime})\penalty\ Z_{I}\right\rangle&\text{else.}\end{cases}

(Y)

Let
t\in[0,\infty)
let
Z^{\prime}=[y_{i}(t)]Z
and
C^{\prime}=y_{i}(t)\,C
. Then

\left\langle\widetilde{Z}^{\prime}(C)\penalty\ Z^{\prime}_{I}\right\rangle\;=\;\begin{cases}\left\langle\widetilde{Z}(C^{\prime})\penalty\ Z_{I}\right\rangle+t\,\left\langle\widetilde{Z}(C^{\prime})\penalty\ Z_{I\cup\{i\}\setminus\{i{\pluscirc}1\}}\right\rangle&\text{if $i\not\in I,i{\pluscirc}1\in I,i\neq\overline{N}$}\\
\left\langle\widetilde{Z}(C^{\prime})\penalty\ Z_{I}\right\rangle+(-1)^{(k-1)}\,t\,\left\langle\widetilde{Z}(C^{\prime})\penalty\ Z_{I\cup\{i\}\setminus\{i{\pluscirc}1\}}\right\rangle&\text{if $i\not\in I,i{\pluscirc}1\in I,i=\overline{N}$}\\
\left\langle\widetilde{Z}(C^{\prime})\penalty\ Z_{I}\right\rangle&\text{else.}\end{cases}

Proof.

The positivity of
Z^{\prime}
in both cases follows from the positivity of
Z
and that we have
s,t\geq 0
, for the same reasons that the nonnegativity of
C
is preserved under
x_{i}
and
y_{i}
. Note that this argument relies on the sign coefficient being
(-1)^{k-1}=(-1)^{k+4-1}
in the overflow case
i=\overline{N}
. Hence, the induced map
\widetilde{Z}^{\prime}
is well-defined from
\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq}_{k,N}
to
\mathrm{Gr}_{k,k+4}
in both cases.

Consider the
x_{i}
case first.
The first
k
rows of the determinant on the left hand side of the equality are
\widetilde{Z}^{\prime}(C)=CZ^{\prime}=C\cdot[x_{i}(t)]Z=(x_{i}(t)\,C)\cdot Z=\widetilde{Z}(x_{i}(t)\,C)=\widetilde{Z}(C^{\prime})
, by the associativity of matrix multiplication. Every row
j\neq i
remains
Z^{\prime}_{j}=Z_{j}
while
Z^{\prime}_{i}=Z_{i}\pm tZ_{i{\pluscirc}1}
, with subtraction if and only if
k
is even and
i=\max{N}
. If
i\not\in I
then clearly
Z_{I}=Z_{I}^{\prime}
and the claim follows. If both
i\in I
and
i{\pluscirc}1\in I
then
Z_{I}
and
Z_{I}^{\prime}
differ by a unimodular row operation, so
\langle Y\penalty\ Z^{\prime}_{I}\rangle=\langle Y\penalty\ Z_{I}\rangle
. The remaining case that
i\in I
and
i{\pluscirc}1\not\in I
gives rise to the additional term
\pm t\,\langle\widetilde{Z}(x_{i}(t)\,C)\penalty\ Z_{I\cup\{i{\pluscirc}1\}\setminus\{i\}}\rangle
by the linearity of the determinant in each row. The argument for
y_{i}
is analogous, where
Z_{i{\pluscirc}1}^{\prime}=Z_{i{\pluscirc}1}\pm tZ_{i}
and the exceptional case is that
i\not\in I
and
i{\pluscirc}1\in I
.
∎

Example 4.25
.

Take
C\in\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq}_{k,n}
and
Z\in\mathrm{Mat}^{>}_{n\times(k+4)}
and
i=3
. Let
Z^{\prime}=[x_{3}(t)]Z
as in the lemma, and denote
Y=\widetilde{Z}^{\prime}(C)=\widetilde{Z}(x_{3}(t)\,C)
.

\displaystyle\langle Y\,Z^{\prime}_{2}\,Z^{\prime}_{5}\,Z^{\prime}_{6}\,Z^{\prime}_{7}\rangle\;=\;\langle Y\,Z_{2}\,Z_{5}\,Z_{6}\,Z_{7}\rangle

\displaystyle\langle Y\,Z^{\prime}_{2}\,Z^{\prime}_{3}\,Z^{\prime}_{4}\,Z^{\prime}_{7}\rangle\;=\;\langle Y\,Z_{2}\,Z_{3}\,Z_{4}\,Z_{7}\rangle

\displaystyle\langle Y\,Z^{\prime}_{2}\,Z^{\prime}_{3}\,Z^{\prime}_{6}\,Z^{\prime}_{7}\rangle\;=\;\langle Y\,Z_{2}\,Z_{3}\,Z_{6}\,Z_{7}\rangle+t\;\langle Y\,Z_{2}\,Z_{4}\,Z_{6}\,Z_{7}\rangle

\displaystyle\langle Y\,Z^{\prime}_{2}\,Z^{\prime}_{4}\,Z^{\prime}_{6}\,Z^{\prime}_{7}\rangle\;=\;\langle Y\,Z_{2}\,Z_{4}\,Z_{6}\,Z_{7}\rangle

As in the previous matrix operations, we state the following useful corollary for functionaries. Unlike the previous cases, here the resulting functionary computed at
Y=\widetilde{Z}(x_{i}(t)C)
depends on the preimages in the Grassmannian via the real parameter
t
. In some cases where we use this lemma,
t
is expressible using
\langle Y\,Z_{I}\rangle
twistors as well.

Lemma 4.26
.

Let
C\in\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq}_{k,N}
,
i\in N
,
and let
F(z_{I}:I\in\scalebox{0.8}{$\tbinom{N}{4}$})
be a homogeneous polynomial such that
F(\langle\widetilde{Z}(C)\penalty\ Z_{I}\rangle)
has a fixed sign.

(X)

Let
s\in(0,\infty),\,C^{\prime}=x_{i}(s)\,C
and let
F^{\prime}
be the polynomial obtained from
F
by the substitution

z_{I}\;\mapsto\;\begin{cases}z_{I}+s\,z_{I\cup\{i{\pluscirc}1\}\setminus\{i\}}&\text{if $i\in I,i{\pluscirc}1\not\in I,i\neq\overline{N}$}\\
z_{I}+(-1)^{(k-1)}\,s\,z_{I\cup\{i{\pluscirc}1\}\setminus\{i\}}&\text{if $i\in I,i{\pluscirc}1\not\in I,i=\overline{N}$}\\
z_{I}&\text{else.}\end{cases}

Then

\SIGN F^{\prime}(\langle\widetilde{Z}(C^{\prime})\;Z_{I}\rangle)\;=\;\SIGN F(\langle\widetilde{Z}(C)\penalty\ Z_{I}\rangle)

Note that if the functionary
F
has a representation in which every twistor that contains
i
in its index set also contains
i{\pluscirc}1
then
F^{\prime}=F
.

(Y)

Let
t\in(0,\infty),\,C^{\prime\prime}=y_{i}(t)\,C
and let
F^{\prime\prime}
be the polynomial obtained from
F
by the substitution

z_{I}\;\mapsto\;\begin{cases}z_{I}+t\,z_{I\cup\{i\}\setminus\{i{\pluscirc}1\}}&\text{if $i\not\in I,i{\pluscirc}1\in I,i\neq\overline{N}$}\\
z_{I}+(-1)^{(k-1)}\,t\,z_{I\cup\{i\}\setminus\{i{\pluscirc}1\}}&\text{if $i\not\in I,i{\pluscirc}1\in I,i=\overline{N}$}\\
z_{I}&\text{else.}\end{cases}

Then

\SIGN F^{\prime\prime}(\langle\widetilde{Z}(C^{\prime\prime})\;Z_{I}\rangle)\;=\;\SIGN F(\langle\widetilde{Z}(C)\penalty\ Z_{I}\rangle)

Note that if the functionary
F
has a representation in which every twistor that contains
i{\pluscirc}1
in its index set also contains
i
then
F^{\prime\prime}=F
.

Proof.

Immediate from Lemma
4.24
.
∎

Remark 4.27
.

For every even
m
, twistors and functionaries of
\mathcal{A}_{n,k,m}(Z)
transform similarly to the above lemmas, under the application of the matrix operations
{\mathrm{pre}}
,
{\mathrm{inc}}
,
x
and
y
to a preimage
C\in\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq}_{k,n}
under
Z\in\mathrm{Mat}^{>}_{n\times(k+m)}
. The situation is different for odd
m
only in the overflow case, where
x_{n}(t)
or
y_{n}(t)
acts on the last and first columns. While the matrix
[x^{k}_{n}(t)]=\mathrm{Id}_{n}+(-1)^{(k-1)}\,t\,\mathrm{E}_{1}^{n}
preserves the nonnegativity of
C
, a different matrix
[x^{k}_{n}(-t)]=\mathrm{Id}_{n}+(-1)^{(k+m-1)}\,t\,\mathrm{E}_{1}^{n}
is required in order to preserve the positivity of
Z
.

We proceed to the matrix operation
\overleftrightarrow{\iota}_{i,l,r}(\textbf{t},\textbf{s})=\overleftrightarrow{\iota}_{i,l,r}(t_{1},\dots,t_{l},s_{1},\dots,s_{r}):\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq}_{k-1,N}\to\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq}_{k,N\cup\{i\}}
, given in Definition
3.6
. Since
\overleftrightarrow{\iota}_{i,l,r}
is a composition of a sequence of
x_{i{\pluscirc}j}
and
y_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}j}
and
{\mathrm{inc}}_{i}
, it transforms functionaries as in the above analysis of these operations. Thus, the resulting functionaries depend on the real parameters
t_{1},\dots,t_{l},s_{1},\dots,s_{r}
. We first analyze the effect of
\overleftrightarrow{\iota}_{i,l,r}
for
m=4
and any
l
and
r
, though later we focus on the case
l+r=4
.

Lemma 4.28
.

Let
i\not\in N
for some finite
N\subset\mathbb{N}
, let
l\geq 0
and
r\geq 0
be such that
l+r\leq|N|
, let
(\textbf{t},\textbf{s})=(t_{1},\dots,t_{l},s_{1},\dots,s_{r})\in(0,\infty)^{l+r}
, and let
F(z_{I}:I\in\scalebox{0.8}{$\tbinom{N}{4}$})
be a homogeneous polynomial.

We denote by
F^{\prime}(z_{I}:I\in\scalebox{0.8}{$\tbinom{N\cup\{i\}}{4}$})
the polynomial obtained from
F
by the following procedure: first substitute
x_{I}\mapsto(-1)^{|I\cap[i]|}\langle Y\penalty\ Z^{\prime}_{I}\rangle
where
Y\in\mathrm{Mat}_{k,k+4}
and
Z^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Mat}^{>}_{(N\cup\{i\})\times[k+4]}
, and then expand multilinearly the twistors
\langle Y\penalty\ Z^{\prime}_{I}\rangle
in terms of
\langle Y\penalty\ Z_{I}\rangle

where

Z^{\prime}\;=\;[x_{i}(s_{1})]\cdot[x_{i{\pluscirc}1}(s_{2})]\cdots[x_{i{\pluscirc}(r-1)}(s_{r})]\cdot[y_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}1}(t_{1})]\cdot[y_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}2}(t_{2})]\cdots[y_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}l}(t_{l})]\cdot Z

so that the
F^{\prime}(\langle Y\penalty\ Z_{I}\rangle:I\in\scalebox{0.8}{$\tbinom{N\cup\{i\}}{4}$})
is the resulting expression. Note that the rows of the matrices
Y,Z^{\prime},Z
are regarded here as formal variables, and that the expansion is carried out by iterating the substitutions from Lemma
4.24
. Note also that
F^{\prime}
does not depend on the parameter
k
except for occasional
(-1)^{k}
.

Then, for every
C\in\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq}_{k-1,N}
and
C^{\prime}=\overleftrightarrow{\iota}_{i,l,r}(\textbf{t},\textbf{s})\,C
, if
F(\langle\widetilde{Z}_{N}(C)\penalty\ Z_{I}\rangle)
has a fixed sign, then

\SIGN F^{\prime}(\langle\widetilde{Z}(C^{\prime})\penalty\ Z_{I}\rangle)\;=\;\SIGN F(\langle\widetilde{Z}_{N}(C)\penalty\ Z_{I}\rangle)

Proof.

Let
Z,Z^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Mat}^{>}_{(N\cup\{i\})\times[k+4]}
be related as in the definition of
F^{\prime}
. Note that the positivity of
Z
implies the positivity of
Z^{\prime}
, since multiplying a positive matrix from the left by any single
[x_{j}(t)]
or
[y_{j}(s)]
preserve positivity, as in the proof of Lemma
4.24
.
By the associativity of matrix multiplication, and the definition of
\overleftrightarrow{\iota}_{i,l,r}
,
we get:

\displaystyle\widetilde{Z}^{\prime}({\mathrm{inc}}_{i}\,C)

\displaystyle\;=\;({\mathrm{inc}}_{i}\,C)Z^{\prime}

\displaystyle\;=\;({\mathrm{inc}}_{i}\,C)[x_{i}(s_{1})]\cdot[x_{i{\pluscirc}1}(s_{2})]\cdots[x_{i{\pluscirc}(r-1)}(s_{r})]\cdot[y_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}1}(t_{1})]\cdot[y_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}2}(t_{2})]\cdots[y_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}l}(t_{l})]\cdot Z

\displaystyle\;=\;(y_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}l}(t_{l})\circ\cdots\circ y_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}1}(t_{1})\,\circ\,x_{i{\pluscirc}(r-1)}(s_{r})\circ\cdots\circ x_{i{\pluscirc}1}(s_{2})\circ x_{i}(s_{1})\,\circ\,{\mathrm{inc}}_{i}(C))\cdot Z

\displaystyle\;=\;\widetilde{Z}(\overleftrightarrow{\iota}_{i,l,r}(\textbf{t},\textbf{s})(C))

and therefore the twistors of
\widetilde{Z}(C^{\prime})
in the lemma satisfy

\langle\widetilde{Z}(C^{\prime})\penalty\ Z^{\prime}_{I}\rangle\;=\;\langle\widetilde{Z}^{\prime}({\mathrm{inc}}_{i}\,C)\penalty\ Z^{\prime}_{I}\rangle.

Therefore,

F\left((-1)^{|I\cap[i]|}\langle\widetilde{Z}\left(C^{\prime}\right)\penalty\ Z^{\prime}_{I}\rangle\right)\;=\;F\left((-1)^{|I\cap[i]|}\langle\widetilde{Z}^{\prime}({\mathrm{inc}}_{i}\,C)\penalty\ Z^{\prime}_{I}\rangle\right)

Combining this equation with Lemma
4.22
and the definition of
F^{\prime}
, and letting
s
be the fixed sign of
F(\langle\widetilde{Z}(C)\penalty\ Z_{I}\rangle)
, or equivalently for this case
F(\langle\widetilde{Z}^{\prime}_{N}(C)\penalty\ Z^{\prime}_{I}\rangle)
, we find that
F^{\prime}(\langle\widetilde{Z}(C^{\prime})\penalty\ Z_{I}\rangle)
has the fixed sign
s
, independently of the arbitrarily chosen
Z
.
∎

Remark 4.29
.

For later reference, we spell out explicitly the relations of rows of
Z^{\prime}
to rows of
Z
in the setting of Lemma
4.28
. These are used below when computing the functionaries that arise from the lemma. For a general
N\times[k+4]
matrix
A
, left multiplication by
x_{i}
and
y_{i}
act on the rows as follows:

\displaystyle([x_{i}(t)]A)_{j}\;

\displaystyle=\;((\mathrm{Id}_{N}+t\,\mathrm{E}_{i}^{i{{\pluscirc}1}})A)_{j}\;=\;(A+t\mathrm{E}_{i}^{i{{\pluscirc}1}}A)_{j}\;=\;\begin{cases}A_{i}+tA_{i{\pluscirc}1}&j=i\\
A_{j}&j\neq i,\end{cases}

\displaystyle([y_{i}(t)]A)_{j}\;

\displaystyle=\;((\mathrm{Id}_{N}+t\,\mathrm{E}_{i{{\pluscirc}1}}^{i})A)_{j}\;=\;(A+t\,\mathrm{E}_{i{{\pluscirc}1}}^{i}A)_{j}\;=\;\begin{cases}A_{i{\pluscirc}1}+tA_{i}&j=i{\pluscirc}1\\
A_{j}&j\neq i{\pluscirc}1,\end{cases}

Therefore, when
[x_{i}(s)]
acts on
Z
, it adds the row
sZ_{i{\pluscirc}1}
to the row
Z_{i}
, and
[y_{i}(t)]
adds the row
tZ_{i}
to the row
Z_{i{\pluscirc}1}
. In the overflow case
i=\max N
, addition is replaced by subtraction if
k
is even.
Therefore, after applying
[x_{i}(s_{1})]\cdot[x_{i{\pluscirc}1}(s_{2})]\cdots[x_{i{\pluscirc}(r-1)}(s_{r})]\cdot[y_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}1}(t_{1})]\cdot[y_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}2}(t_{2})]\cdots[y_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}l}(t_{l})]
on
Z
,

\displaystyle Z^{\prime}_{j}\;=\;Z_{j}

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!j\not\in\{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}(l-1),\dots,i{\pluscirc}(r-1)\}

\displaystyle Z^{\prime}_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}j}\;=\;Z_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}j}\;\pm\;t_{j+1}\,Z^{\prime}_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}(j+1)}

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!0<j<l

\displaystyle Z^{\prime}_{i{\pluscirc}j}\;=\;Z_{i{\pluscirc}j}\;\pm\;s_{j+1}\,Z^{\prime}_{i{\pluscirc}(j+1)}

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!0<j<r

\displaystyle Z^{\prime}_{i}\;=\;Z_{i}\;\pm\;t_{1}\,Z^{\prime}_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}1}\;\pm\;s_{1}\,Z^{\prime}_{i{\pluscirc}1}

where the
\pm
sign is
+
in the consecutive case, and
(-1)^{k-1}
in the overflow case, i.e., when the two row indices are
\min(N\cup\{i\})
and
\max(N\cup\{i\})
. The formula for
Z^{\prime}_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}j}
follows from the application of
[y_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}(j+1)}(t_{j+1})]
on
Z
. The added term is specifically
Z^{\prime}_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}(j+1)}
rather than
Z_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}(j+1)}
because of a previous application of
[y_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}(j+2)}(t_{j+2})]
. The formula for
Z^{\prime}_{i{\pluscirc}j}
similarly follows from
[x_{i{\pluscirc}j}(s_{j+1})]
. The case of
Z^{\prime}_{i}
follows from both
[x_{i}(s_{1})]
and
[y_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}1}(t_{1})]
.

Iterating these recursive relations, we expand every row
Z_{j}^{\prime}
as a linear combination of the rows of
Z
as follows:

\displaystyle Z^{\prime}_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}l}\;=\;Z_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}l}

\displaystyle Z^{\prime}_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}(l-1)}\;=\;Z_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}(l-1)}\;\pm\;t_{l}\,Z_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}l}

\displaystyle Z^{\prime}_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}(l-2)}\;=\;Z_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}(l-2)}\;\pm\;t_{l-1}\,Z_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}(l-1)}\;\pm\;t_{l-1}t_{l}\,Z_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}l}

\displaystyle\;\;\;\;\;\;\vdots

\displaystyle Z^{\prime}_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}1}\;=\;Z_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}1}\;\pm\;t_{2}\,Z_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}2}\;\pm\;t_{2}t_{3}\,Z_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}3}\;\pm\dots\pm\;(t_{2}t_{3}\cdots t_{l})\,Z_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}l}

where the sign of the term
\pm Z_{p}
in the expansion of
Z_{q}^{\prime}
is
+
unless
k
is even and
p>q
. Similarly,

\displaystyle Z^{\prime}_{i{\pluscirc}r}\;=\;Z_{i{\pluscirc}r}

\displaystyle Z^{\prime}_{i{\pluscirc}(r-1)}\;=\;Z_{i{\pluscirc}(r-1)}\;\pm\;s_{r}\,Z_{i{\pluscirc}r}

\displaystyle Z^{\prime}_{i{\pluscirc}(r-2)}\;=\;Z_{i{\pluscirc}(r-2)}\;\pm\;s_{r-1}\,Z_{i{\pluscirc}(r-1)}\;\pm\;s_{r-1}s_{r}\,Z_{i{\pluscirc}r}

\displaystyle\;\;\;\;\;\;\vdots

\displaystyle Z^{\prime}_{i{\pluscirc}1}\;=\;Z_{i{\pluscirc}1}\;\pm\;s_{2}\,Z_{i{\pluscirc}2}\;\pm\;s_{2}s_{3}\,Z_{i{\pluscirc}3}\;\pm\dots\pm\;(s_{2}s_{3}\cdots s_{r})\,Z_{i{\pluscirc}r}

where the sign of the term
\pm Z_{p}
in the expansion of
Z_{q}^{\prime}
is
+
unless
k
is even and
p<q
. Finally,

\displaystyle Z^{\prime}_{i}\;=\;Z_{i}

\displaystyle\;\pm\;t_{1}\,Z_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}1}\;\pm\;t_{1}t_{2}\,Z_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}2}\;\pm\;\dots\;\pm\;(t_{1}t_{2}\cdots t_{l})\,Z_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}l}

\displaystyle\;\pm\;s_{1}Z_{i{\pluscirc}1}\;\pm\;s_{1}s_{2}\,Z_{i{\pluscirc}2}\;\pm\;\dots\;\pm\;(s_{1}s_{2}\cdots s_{r})\,Z_{i{\pluscirc}r}\;\;\;\;\;\;

where
\pm
is
(-1)^{k-1}
for terms with index
i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}j>i
or
i{\pluscirc}j<i
, and
+
otherwise.

Expanding each twistor using these combinations for its four
Z_{I}^{\prime}
rows, we obtain a functionary of
\overleftrightarrow{\iota}_{i,l,r}(\textbf{t},\textbf{s})(C)
with respect to
Z
as claimed above.

Remark 4.30
.

Let
I=\{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4}\}\subset N\cup\{i\}
. In the setting of Lemma
4.28
, we point to some cases in which the expansion of
\langle Y\penalty\ Z^{\prime}_{I}\rangle
in the twistors
\langle Y\penalty\ Z_{J}\rangle
from Remark
4.29
can be simplified.

1.

If
\{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}p,i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}q\}\subset I
for
0\leq p<q\leq l
, truncate the expansion of
Z^{\prime}_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}p}
from the term
Z_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}q}
.

2.

If
\{i{\pluscirc}p,i{\pluscirc}q\}\subset I
for
0\leq p<q\leq r
, truncate the expansion of
Z^{\prime}_{i{\pluscirc}p}
from the term
Z_{i{\pluscirc}q}
.

3.

If
\{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}p,i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}(p+1)\}\subset I
for
0<p<l
, then replace
Z_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}p}^{\prime}
by
Z_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}p}
.

4.

If
\{i{\pluscirc}p,i{\pluscirc}(p+1)\}\subset I
for
0<p<r
, then replace
Z_{i{\pluscirc}p}^{\prime}
by
Z_{i{\pluscirc}p}
.

5.

If
\{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}l,\dots,i,\dots,i{\pluscirc}r\}\setminus I=\{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}l^{\prime},\dots,i,\dots,i{\pluscirc}r^{\prime}\}
for some
l^{\prime},r^{\prime}\geq 0
then
\langle Y\penalty\ Z^{\prime}_{I}\rangle=\langle Y\penalty\ Z_{I}\rangle
.

Proof.

The first two cases follow from the general formulas for
Z_{j}^{\prime}
in Remark
4.29
, noting that twistors where
Z^{\prime}_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}q}
or
Z^{\prime}_{i{\pluscirc}q}
appears twice must vanish. The remaining are special cases.
∎

The coefficients of the polynomial
F^{\prime}(z_{I})
from Lemma
4.28
depend on the variables
t_{1},\dots,t_{l},s_{1},\dots,s_{r}
as a homogeneous polynomial in
z_{I}
.
A key step in our analysis of the transformation of functionaries under
\overleftrightarrow{\iota}_{i,l,r}
is that when
l+r\leq 4
these variables can generically be recovered from the twistor coordinates of
Y
, and thus we can express the entire functionary
F^{\prime}(\langle\widetilde{Z}(C^{\prime})\penalty\ Z_{I}\rangle)
from Lemma
4.28
only with twistors, eliminating the real numbers
(\textbf{t},\textbf{s})
.

Lemma 4.31
.

Let
C=\overleftrightarrow{\iota}_{i,l,r}(\textbf{t},\textbf{s})\,C^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq}_{k,N}
for some
(\textbf{t},\textbf{s})=(t_{1},\dots,t_{l},s_{1},\dots,s_{r})\in\mathbb{R}^{l+r}
and some
C^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq}_{k-1,N\setminus\{i\}}
such that
i\in N
and
l+r\leq 4
. Let
J=\{j_{1},j_{2},j_{3},j_{4},j_{5}\}\subseteq N
be such that
\{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}l,\dots,i,\dots,i{\pluscirc}r\}\subseteq J
. Then, for every
Z\in\mathrm{Mat}^{>}_{N\times[k+4]}
,

\displaystyle t_{j}\;

\displaystyle=\;\frac{-\left\langle CZ\penalty\ Z_{J\setminus\{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}j\}}\right\rangle}{\left\langle CZ\penalty\ Z_{J\setminus\{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}(j-1)\}}\right\rangle}\;(-1)^{(k-1)\delta[i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}j=\overline{N}]}

\displaystyle 1\leq j\leq l

\displaystyle s_{j}\;

\displaystyle=\;\frac{-\left\langle CZ\penalty\ Z_{J\setminus\{i{\pluscirc}j\}}\right\rangle}{\left\langle CZ\penalty\ Z_{J\setminus\{i{\pluscirc}(j-1)\}}\right\rangle}\;(-1)^{(k-1)\delta[i{\pluscirc}j=\underline{N}]}

\displaystyle 1\leq j\leq r

as long as the denominators are nonzero.

These formulas follow by comparing the two determinants, noting that one row of
CZ
gives essentially
Z_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}(j-1)}+t_{j}\,Z_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}j}
. We omit further details, and instead state the following lemma, which formulates the relation between variables and twistors suitably for our purposes.

Lemma 4.32
.

Let
C=\overleftrightarrow{\iota}_{i,l,4-l}(\textbf{t},\textbf{s})\,C^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq}_{k,N}
for some
C^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq}_{k-1,N\setminus\{i\}}
where
i\in N
and
(\textbf{t},\textbf{s})=(t_{1},\dots,t_{l},s_{1},\dots,s_{4-l})\in(0,\infty)^{4}
. Denote
J=\{j_{1},j_{2},j_{3},j_{4},j_{5}\}=\{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}l,\dots,i,\dots,i{\pluscirc}(4-l)\}
in this order. If
j_{1}<j_{5}
then for every
Z\in\mathrm{Mat}^{>}_{N\times[k+4]}
the vector

\left(+\langle\widetilde{Z}(C)\penalty\ Z_{J\setminus\{j_{1}\}}\rangle,\,-\langle\widetilde{Z}(C)\penalty\ Z_{J\setminus\{j_{2}\}}\rangle,\,+\langle\widetilde{Z}(C)\penalty\ Z_{J\setminus\{j_{3}\}}\rangle,-\langle\widetilde{Z}(C)\penalty\ Z_{J\setminus\{j_{4}\}}\rangle,+\langle\widetilde{Z}(C)\penalty\ Z_{J\setminus\{j_{5}\}}\rangle\right)

is a nonnegative scalar multiple of the vector

\Big((t_{1}t_{2}\cdots t_{l}),\,\dots,\,t_{1}t_{2},\,t_{1},\,1,\,s_{1},\,s_{1}s_{2},\,\dots,\,(s_{1}s_{2}\cdots s_{4-l})\Big)

If
j_{5}<j_{1}
then the same holds where each
\pm\langle\widetilde{Z}(C)\penalty\ Z_{J\setminus\{j_{h}\}}\rangle
is multiplied by
(-1)^{k\,|\{\underline{N},\dots,j_{5}\}\setminus\{j_{h}\}|}
.

The proof of Lemma
4.32
appears later, after Lemma
4.35
.

Example 4.33
.

On the image of
\widetilde{Z}\circ\overleftrightarrow{\iota}_{5,2,2}(t_{1},t_{2},s_{1},s_{2})
in
\mathcal{A}_{7,k,4}(Z)
,

(+\langle 4567\rangle,-\langle 3567\rangle,+\langle 3467\rangle,-\langle 3457\rangle,+\langle 3456\rangle)\;\;\propto\;\;(t_{1}t_{2},t_{1},1,s_{1},s_{1}s_{2})

Example 4.34
.

On the image of
\widetilde{Z}\circ\overleftrightarrow{\iota}_{1,3,1}(t_{1},t_{2},t_{3},s_{1})
in
\mathcal{A}_{7,k,4}(Z)
,

(+\langle 1267\rangle,-\langle 1257\rangle,+\langle 1256\rangle,-(-1)^{k}\langle 2567\rangle,+(-1)^{k}\langle 1567\rangle)\;\;\propto\;\;(t_{1}t_{2}t_{3},t_{1}t_{2},t_{1},1,s_{1})

Specializing Lemma
4.32
even to one coordinate, it already shows that certain twistors are either nonnegative or nonpositive on some parts of the amplituhedron, such as
\langle 3567\rangle
,
\langle 3457\rangle
,
\langle 1257\rangle
, and
\langle 2567\rangle
in the above examples. The other twistors in these examples have a constant sign on the amplituhedron by Lemma
4.13
. We remark that Lemma
4.32
easily generalizes to all even
m
, with
\overleftrightarrow{\iota}_{i,l,m-l}
and
|J|=m+1
, and also to odd
m
in the case
j_{1}<j_{m+1}
.

Lemma 4.35
.

Let
k,m\geq 1
and let
Y\in\mathrm{Mat}^{\ast}_{k\times(k+m)}
and
Z\in\mathrm{Mat}^{\ast}_{(m+1)\times(k+m)}
be two matrices of full rank. If at least one of the
m+1
determinants

\langle Y\,Z_{2}\,Z_{3}\,\cdots\,Z_{m+1}\rangle,\penalty\ \langle Y\,Z_{1}\,Z_{3}\,\cdots\,Z_{m+1}\rangle,\penalty\ \ldots,\penalty\ \langle Y\,Z_{1}\,Z_{2}\,\cdots\,Z_{m}\rangle

is nonzero, then
\mathrm{span}(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{k})\cap\mathrm{span}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{m+1})
is a one-dimensional space, spanned by

\sum_{j=1}^{m+1}(-1)^{j-1}\,\langle Y\,Z_{1}\,\cdots\,Z_{j-1}\,Z_{j+1}\,\cdots\,Z_{m+1}\rangle\,Z_{j}

Proof.

Since one of the
m+1
determinants is nonzero, the space
\mathrm{span}(Y_{1},\dots,Y_{k},Z_{1},\dots,Z_{m+1})
is of full dimension
k+m
. The matrices
Y
and
Z
have full ranks
k
and
m+1
respectively, and it follows that the subspace
\mathrm{span}(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{k})\cap\mathrm{span}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{m+1})
is exactly one-dimensional. Consider a nonzero vector in this intersection, which is unique up to scaling:

\sum_{i=1}^{k}a_{i}Y_{i}\;=\;\sum_{j=1}^{m+1}b_{j}Z_{j}

Let
q\in[m+1]
be such that
\langle Y\,Z_{1},\cdots\,Z_{q-1}\,Z_{q+1}\,\cdots\,Z_{m+1}\rangle\neq 0
, as we assumed to exist.
Observe that
b_{q}\neq 0,
since otherwise
\mathrm{span}(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{k})\cap\mathrm{span}(Z_{1},\ldots,\,Z_{q-1}\,Z_{q+1}\,\ldots\ Z_{m+1})\neq\{0\}.

For an arbitrary
p\in[m+1]
, apply to both sides the linear functional
f_{pq}(u)=\langle Y\,Z_{[m+1]\setminus\{p,q\}}\,u\rangle
. Since
f_{pq}(Y_{i})=0
for every
i\in[k]
, and
f_{pq}(Z_{j})=0
for every
j\not\in\{p,q\}
,

0\;=\;b_{p}\,f_{pq}(Z_{p})+b_{q}f_{pq}(Z_{q}).

Hence, the coefficients
b_{1},\dots,b_{m+1}
satisfy that for any
p
,

b_{p}\;=\;-\frac{f_{pq}(Z_{q})}{f_{pq}(Z_{p})}b_{q}\;=\;-\frac{\langle Y\,Z_{[m+1]\setminus\{p,q\}}\,Z_{q}\rangle}{\langle Y\,Z_{[m+1]\setminus\{p,q\}}\,Z_{p}\rangle}b_{q}\;=\;(-1)^{p-q}\,\frac{\langle Y\,Z_{[m+1]\setminus\{p\}}\rangle}{\langle Y\,Z_{[m+1]\setminus\{q\}}\rangle}b_{q}.

By multiplying the resulting vector by
\langle Y\,Z_{[m+1]\setminus\{q\}}\rangle
, which is nonzero, the claim follows.

∎

Proof of Lemma
4.32
.

First consider the case
j_{1}<j_{5}
. The point
C\in\mathrm{Gr}_{k,N}
is as usual regarded as a nonnegative representative
k\times N
matrix. Since
C
arises from
\overleftrightarrow{\iota}_{i,l,4-l}
it contains a row
C_{k}
with the five cyclically consecutive nonzero entries in the index set
J
,

C_{k}^{J}\;=\;\Big((t_{1}t_{2}\cdots t_{l}),\,\dots,\,t_{1}t_{2},\,t_{1},\,1,\,s_{1},\,s_{1}s_{2},\,\dots,\,(s_{1}s_{2}\cdots s_{4-l})\Big),

with all other entries being zero.
Therefore, after right multiplication by
Z
, the image
Y=\widetilde{Z}(C)
contains the corresponding row

Y_{k}\;=\;(CZ)_{k}\;=\;C_{k}^{j_{1}}Z_{j_{1}}+C_{k}^{j_{2}}Z_{j_{2}}+C_{k}^{j_{3}}Z_{j_{3}}+C_{k}^{j_{4}}Z_{j_{4}}+C_{k}^{j_{5}}Z_{j_{5}}\;\in\;\mathbb{R}^{k+4}

where
(Z_{i})_{i\in N}
are the rows of
Z
. This shows that
Y_{k}\in\mathrm{span}(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{k})\cap\mathrm{span}(Z_{j_{1}},\ldots,Z_{j_{5}})
, and it is nonzero since
Y
is in the Grassmannian.

The
5\times(k+4)
matrix
Z_{J}
has a full rank by the positivity of
Z
, and so does the
k\times(k+4)
matrix
Y
. The vector
Y_{k}
is proportional to the combination of rows
Z_{i}
provided by Lemma
4.35
. Therefore, the five twistors

\Big({+}\langle Y\,j_{2}\,j_{3}\,j_{4}\,j_{5}\rangle_{Z},\,-\langle Y\,j_{1}\,j_{3}\,j_{4}\,j_{5}\rangle_{Z},\,+\langle Y\,j_{1}\,j_{2}\,j_{4}\,j_{5}\rangle_{Z},\,-\langle Y\,j_{1}\,j_{2}\,j_{3}\,j_{5}\rangle_{Z},\,+\langle Y\,j_{1}\,j_{2}\,j_{3}\,j_{4}\rangle_{Z}\Big)

are proportional to the coefficients
C_{k}^{j_{1}},\dots,C_{k}^{j_{5}}
. Even if these twistors all vanish, then the required claim holds as well, where the proportion equals zero. Otherwise, the proportion is positive because the first term
(t_{1}\cdots t_{l})>0
while the first twistor
\langle Y\,j_{2}\,j_{3}\,j_{4}\,j_{5}\rangle
is nonnegative by Lemma
4.13
.

The overflow case
j_{5}<j_{1}
is similar with some sign adjustments. In exactly one of the two intervals
J^{\prime}=\{j_{1},\dots,\overline{N}\}
or
J^{\prime\prime}=\{\underline{N},\dots,j_{5}\}
, the coefficients
C_{k}^{j_{h}}
gain a factor of
(-1)^{k-1}
due to an
x_{j}
or
y_{j}
operation. Thus, by Lemma
4.35
, the vector
(C_{k}^{j_{1}},\dots,C_{k}^{j_{5}})
is proportional to the same five twistors with some
\pm
signs, that still alternate within each of the two parts. The row reordering that rewrites
\langle Y\,j_{1}\,\cdots,\,j_{h-1}\,j_{h+1}\,\cdots\,j_{5}\rangle
as
\langle Y\,J\setminus\{j_{h}\}\rangle
contributes another
(-1)
factor to the twistors in either
J^{\prime}
or
J^{\prime\prime}
, depending on parity. Note that the parity of
|J^{\prime\prime}\setminus\{j_{h}\}|
detects exactly whether
j_{h}\in J^{\prime}
or
J^{\prime\prime}
. In conclusion, the ratio
(-1)^{h-1}(-1)^{k|J^{\prime\prime}\setminus\{j_{h}\}|}\langle Y\,J\setminus\{j_{h}\}\rangle\,/\,C_{k}^{j_{h}}
is equal to a single proportionality constant for all
h\in\{1,2,3,4,5\}
, which is again nonnegative by the two cases of Lemma
4.13
.
∎

We return to the setting of Lemma
4.28
, which analyzes the evolution of a functionary
F
under the matrix operation
\overleftrightarrow{\iota}_{i,l,r}(t_{1},\dots,s_{r})
. In the case
l+r=4
, the output matrix has a row with five nonzero entries, and one can apply Lemma
4.32
in order to decouple the resulting functionary from the apparent dependence on the parameters
t_{1},\dots,s_{4-l}
.
The following lemma gives formulas that generically let us express this functionary only in terms of twistors of the image under
\widetilde{Z}
.

Lemma 4.36
.

Let
Y=\widetilde{Z}(\overleftrightarrow{\iota}_{i,l,4-l}(\textbf{t},\textbf{s})\;C)
for some
C\in\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq}_{k-1,N\setminus\{i\}}
where
i\not\in N
,
Z\in\mathrm{Mat}^{>}_{N\times[k+4]}
and
(\textbf{t},\textbf{s})=(t_{1},\dots,t_{l},s_{1},\dots,s_{4-l})\in(0,\infty)^{4}
. Then, the rows of the matrix

Z^{\prime}\;=\;[x_{i}(s_{1})]\cdot[x_{i{\pluscirc}1}(s_{2})]\cdots[x_{i{\pluscirc}(3-l)}(s_{4-l})]\cdot[y_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}1}(t_{1})]\cdot[y_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}2}(t_{2})]\cdots[y_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}l}(t_{l})]\cdot Z

satisfy the following formulas, regardless of the nonnegative variables
(t_{1},\dots,t_{l},s_{1},\dots,s_{4-l})
.

1.

If
j=i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}l
or
j=i{\pluscirc}(4-l)
or
j\not\in J=\{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}l,\dots,i{\pluscirc}(4-l)\}
then
Z^{\prime}_{j}=Z_{j}
.

2.

If
i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}l\leq j\leq i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}1
then

\langle Y\penalty\ Z_{J\setminus\{j\}}\rangle\,Z^{\prime}_{j}\;=\;\langle Y\penalty\ Z_{J\setminus\{j\}}\rangle\,Z_{j}\;-\;\langle Y\penalty\ Z_{J\setminus\{j{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}1\}}\rangle\,Z_{j{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}1}\;+\;\dots\;\pm\;\langle Y\penalty\ Z_{J\setminus\{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}l\}}\rangle\,Z_{i{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}l}

3.

If
i{\pluscirc}1\leq j\leq i{\pluscirc}(4-l)
then

\langle Y\penalty\ Z_{J\setminus\{j\}}\rangle\,Z^{\prime}_{j}\;=\;\langle Y\penalty\ Z_{J\setminus\{j\}}\rangle\,Z_{j}\;-\;\langle Y\penalty\ Z_{J\setminus\{j{\pluscirc}1\}}\rangle\,Z_{j{\pluscirc}1}\;+\;\dots\;\pm\;\langle Y\penalty\ Z_{J\setminus\{i{\pluscirc}(4-l)\}}\rangle\,Z_{i{\pluscirc}(4-l)}

Proof.

These relations follow immediately from Remark
4.29
following the proof of Lemma
4.28
. The case
Z_{j}^{\prime}=Z_{j}
is only restated. The second case is obtained by replacing the coefficients
1,t_{j+1},t_{j+1}t_{j+2},\dots
with proportional twistors, supplied by Lemma
4.32
. The third case is obtained similarly by replacing the coefficients
1,s_{j+1},s_{j+1}s_{j+2},\dots,(s_{j+1}\cdots s_{4-l})
.

We note that a combined formula holds for
Z_{i}^{\prime}
and it is omitted from this lemma. Also the overflow cases are similar with possible sign adjustments, as derived in Remark
4.29
and the proof of
4.32
, and not repeated here.
∎

The following procedure, named
promotion
, summarizes how Lemma
4.28
together with Lemma
4.36
enable us to analyze the effect of
\overleftrightarrow{\iota}_{i,l,4-l}
on twistors and functionaries.

Definition 4.37

(Promotion under
\overleftrightarrow{\iota}_{i,l,4-l}
)
.

The
promotion
of a functionary
F(\langle YZ_{I}\rangle:I\in\scalebox{0.8}{$\tbinom{N}{4}$})
under the embedding
\overleftrightarrow{\iota}_{i,l,4-l}
is the outcome of the following sequence of operations:

1.

Substitute
\langle YZ_{I}\rangle\mapsto(-1)^{|I\cap[i]|}\left\langle YZ^{\prime}_{I}\right\rangle
for every twistor in
F
,
as in Lemma
4.28
.

2.

Express each
Z^{\prime}_{j}
as a combination of
Z_{r}
-s, with coefficients ratios of twistors, as in Lemma
4.36
.

3.

Expand multilinearly each of the above twistors
\langle YZ^{\prime}_{I}\rangle
to a rational function in
\langle YZ_{I}\rangle
.

4.

Multiply the resulting rational function by the greatest common divisor of the denominators.

This yields a functionary
F^{\prime}(\langle YZ_{I}\rangle:I\in\scalebox{0.8}{$\tbinom{N\cup\{i\}}{4}$})
which is the
promotion
of
F
.

Using Lemmas
4.28
and Lemma
4.36
, we can deduce that the a functionary
F
of fixed sign at
C
promotes to a fixed-sign functionary
F^{\prime}
at
C^{\prime}=\overleftrightarrow{\iota}_{i,l,4-l}(\textbf{t},\textbf{s})\,C
. This requires that the cleared denominators in step (4) also have a fixed sign. Usually, Lemma
4.13
and Lemma
4.32
come to the rescue, and thus the sign of
F^{\prime}
at
C^{\prime}
is fixed and computable. This is demonstrated in the next section in the special cases
\overleftrightarrow{\iota}_{i,3,1}(\textbf{t},\textbf{s})
and
\overleftrightarrow{\iota}_{n-2,2,2}(\textbf{t},\textbf{s})
which are most relevant to our purposes, though the method of sign-preserving promotion is applicable to
\overleftrightarrow{\iota}_{i,l,r}
all
i,l
and
r
and even other
m
.

Remark 4.38
.

As operations (1)-(4) above are homogeneous in indices, if
F
is pure then so is the resulting functionary.

In conclusion, the results of this section allow us to compute new fixed-sign functionaries from given ones. Given a pure functionary
F
of fixed sign at some point
C
,
Lemmas
4.20
,
4.22
,
4.26
, and
4.28
help us find a functionary
F^{\prime}
of fixed sign at the image of
C
under the matrix operations
{\mathrm{pre}}_{i}
,
{\mathrm{inc}}_{i}
,
x_{i}
,
y_{i}
, and
\overleftrightarrow{\iota}_{i,l,4-l}(\textbf{t},\textbf{s})
. For the latter case, Lemmas
4.31
,
4.32
and
4.36
give us tools to derive such a functionary which does not depend on the parameters
(\mathbf{t},\mathbf{s})
, which we refer to as the promotion of
F
.

4.3
Upper and Lower Promotion

Recall the two matrix operations specializing
\overleftrightarrow{\iota}_{i,l,4-l}(\textbf{t},\textbf{s})
, the upper embedding
\overleftrightarrow{\;\textsc{upp}\;}_{j}=\overleftrightarrow{\iota}_{j,3,1}
and the lower embedding
\overleftrightarrow{\;\textsc{low}\;}_{n-2}=\overleftrightarrow{\iota}_{n-2,2,2}
from Definitions
3.7
-
3.8
. In this section, we demonstrate Definition
4.37
and analyze the evolution of functionaries under these operations, named
upper promotion
and
lower promotion
respectively. We start from the following explicit description.

Proposition 4.39

(Upper and lower promotion)
.

Let
1\leq i<a<b<c<d\leq n
. The promotion of the twistor
\langle a\,b\,c\,d\rangle
under
\overleftrightarrow{\;\textsc{upp}\;}_{i}
is given by the following functionary:

•

if
d\notin\{n-1,n\}
then it remains
\langle a\,b\,c\,d\rangle

•

if
d=n-1
and
c<n-1
, then

\langle a\,b\,c\,n{-}1\rangle\;\langle i\,i{+}1\,n{-}2\,n\rangle-\langle a\,b\,c\,n{-}2\rangle\;\langle i\,i{+}1\,n{-}1\,n\rangle

•

if
d=n
and
c<n-1
, then

\langle a\;b\;c\;n\rangle\;\langle i\;i{+}1\;n{-}2\;n{-}1\rangle-\langle a\;b\;c\;n{-}1\rangle\;\langle i\;i{+}1\;n{-}2\;n\rangle+\langle a\;b\;c\;n{-}2\rangle\;\langle i\;i{+}1\;n{-}1\;n\rangle

•

if
d=n
and
c=n-1
, then

\langle i\,i{+}1\,n{-}2\,n{-}1\rangle\left(\langle a\,b\,n{-}1\,n\rangle\;\langle i\,i{+}1\,n{-}2\,n\rangle-\langle a\,b\,n-2\,n\rangle\;\langle i\,i{+}1\,n{-}1\,n\rangle\right)

We call this functionary the
upper promotion
at
i
of the twistor
\langle a\,b\,c\,d\rangle
.
The
upper promotion
at
i
of a pure functionary
F
on the index set
\{i+1,\dots,n\}
is the result of replacing each twistor in
F
with its upper promotion at
i
.

Let
a<b<c<d
be elements of
\{1,\dots,i,i+1,n-1,n\}
where
i+1<n-2
. The promotion of
\langle a\penalty\ b\penalty\ c\penalty\ d\rangle
under
\overleftrightarrow{\;\textsc{low}\;}_{n-2}
is given by the following functionary:

•

if
i+1\notin\{c,d\}
, then it is
\langle a\penalty\ b\penalty\ c\penalty\ d\rangle

•

if
c=i+1
and
d=n
, then

\langle a\penalty\ b\penalty\ i{+}1\penalty\ d\rangle\;\langle i\penalty\ n{-}2\penalty\ n{-}1\penalty\ n\rangle-\langle a\penalty\ b\penalty\ i\penalty\ d\rangle\;\langle i{+}1\penalty\ n{-}2\penalty\ n{-}1\penalty\ n\rangle

•

if
d=i+1
, then

\langle a\penalty\ b\penalty\ c\penalty\ i{+}1\rangle\;\langle i\penalty\ n{-}2\penalty\ n{-}1\penalty\ n\rangle-\langle a\penalty\ b\penalty\ c\penalty\ i\rangle\;\langle i{+}1\penalty\ n{-}2\penalty\ n{-}1\penalty\ n\rangle

•

We forgo the case where
n-1
appears in the twistor, to avoid unnecessary complicated expressions.

We call this functionary the
lower promotion at
n-2
of
\langle a\penalty\ b\penalty\ c\penalty\ d\rangle
. The
lower promotion
of a pure functionary on the index set
\{1,\dots,i+1,n\}
is the result of replacing each twistor in
F
with its lower promotion.

Proof.

The upper promotion at
i
of a functionary
F
, specialized from Definition
4.37
, is performed by doing the following operations:

•

in all twistors, substitute
Z_{n}
with

Z_{n}-\frac{\langle i\;i{+}1\;n{-}2\;n\rangle}{\langle i\;i{+}1\;n{-}2\;n{-}1\rangle}Z_{n-1}+\frac{\langle i\;i{+}1\;n{-}1\;n\rangle}{\langle i\;i{+}1\;n{-}2\;n{-}1\rangle}Z_{n-2}
and
Z_{n-1}
with

Z_{n-1}-\frac{\langle i\,i{+}1\,n{-}1\,n\rangle}{\langle i\,i{+}1\,n{-}2\,n\rangle}Z_{n-2}
.

•

multiply the functionary by the common denominator
\langle i\;i{+}1\;n{-}2\;n{-}1\rangle^{d_{n}(F)}
, where
d_{j}(F)
is the multiplicity of
j
in
F
as in Definition
4.5
.

•

multiply the functionary by the common denominator
\langle i\,i{+}1\,n{-}2\,n\rangle^{d_{n-1}(F)}
.

Now we can expand linearly each twistor containing
n
or
n-1
to get a rational function. The denominators will cancel with the multiplicative factors, yielding a polynomial, i.e. a functionary.
It is straightforward to verify the equivalence of applying these three operations and applying the four cases in the proposition: any twistor where
Z_{n-1}
or
Z_{n}
is included must be fall into one of the 4 cases of upper promotion at Proposition
4.39
. In the first 3 options there is up to one substitution, which is exactly as in the 3 alternative substitutions above (including the multiplication by the common denominator). In the last case we substitute both
Z_{n}
and
Z_{n-1}
as above, and after linear expansion and reordering of the twistor coordinates
\langle a\;b\;n-1\;n\rangle
becomes

\langle a\;b\;n-1\;n\rangle+\langle a\;b\;n-2\;n\rangle\left(-\frac{\langle i\,i{+}1\,n{-}1\,n\rangle}{\langle i\,i{+}1\,n{-}2\,n\rangle}\right)\\
+\langle\;a\;b\;n-1\;n-2\rangle\left(\frac{\langle i\,i{+}1\,n{-}1\,n\rangle}{\langle i\,i{+}1\,n{-}2\,n\rangle}\cdot\frac{\langle i\;i{+}1\;n{-}2\;n\rangle}{\langle i\;i{+}1\;n{-}2\;n{-}1\rangle}-\frac{\langle i\;i{+}1\;n{-}1\;n\rangle}{\langle i\;i{+}1\;n{-}2\;n{-}1\rangle}\right)

which after multiplying by the common denominators
\langle i\;i{+}1\;n{-}2\;n{-}1\rangle,\langle i\,i{+}1\,n{-}2\,n\rangle
gives the desired expression.

For the lower embedding (Definition
3.8
), Definition
4.37
specializes to performing the following operations.
We present it in a slightly more general context, in which the functionary may include the index
n-1
in some twistor.

•

in all twistors, substitute
Z_{i+1}\mapsto Z_{i+1}-\frac{\langle i{+}1\penalty\ n{-}2\penalty\ n{-}1\penalty\ n\rangle}{\langle i\penalty\ n{-}2\penalty\ n{-}1\penalty\ n\rangle}Z_{i}
and
Z_{n-1}\mapsto Z_{n-1}-\frac{\langle i\penalty\ i{+}1\penalty\ n{-}2\penalty\ n{-}1\rangle}{\langle i\penalty\ i{+}1\penalty\ n{-}2\penalty\ n\rangle}Z_{n}.

•

multiply the functionary by the common denominator
{\langle i\penalty\ n{-}2\penalty\ n{-}1\penalty\ n\rangle}^{d_{i+1}(F)}
, where
j
is the degree of
i+1
in the pure functionary
F
.

•

multiply the functionary by the common denominator
{\langle i\penalty\ i{+}1\penalty\ n{-}2\penalty\ n\rangle}^{d_{i}(F)}
, where
j
is the degree of
n-1
in the pure functionary
F
.

It is again straightforward to see that applying these three operations is equivalent to applying the three cases in the proposition.
∎

Remark 4.40
.

Functionary promotion for both types of embedding is programmed in the companion Sage library for this paper
[
ELT22
]
.

Our goal is to show that upper and lower promotions of fixed-sign functionaries yield new fixed-sign functionaries.
As a warm-up, it is helpful to illustrate this phenomenon with a specific example.

Example 4.41
.

Let
C^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq}_{k-1,234567}
and suppose that the functionary
\langle 3\,4\,5\,7\rangle
has a fixed sign
+1
at
C^{\prime}
. This means that
\langle\widetilde{Z}(C^{\prime})\,Z_{3}\,Z_{4}\,Z_{5}\,Z_{7}\rangle>0
for all
Z\in\mathrm{Mat}^{>}_{234567\times[k+3]}
, which is for example the case if it arises from
C^{\prime}={\mathrm{pre}}_{6}\,C^{\prime\prime}
, by Lemmas
4.13
and
4.20
. Suppose also that we are interested in the upper embedding
C=\overleftrightarrow{\;\textsc{upp}\;}_{1}(t,t^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},s)\,C^{\prime}
for all
(t,t^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},s)\in(0,\infty)^{4}
.

Lemma
4.28
says that the functionary in
C,Z
equal everywhere to
\langle\widetilde{Z}(C)\,Z^{\prime}_{3}\,Z^{\prime}_{4}\,Z^{\prime}_{5}\,Z^{\prime}_{7}\rangle
is a functionary of fixed sign
+1
. We can express this functionary by expanding
\langle\widetilde{Z}(C)\,Z^{\prime}_{3}\,Z^{\prime}_{4}\,Z^{\prime}_{5}\,Z^{\prime}_{7}\rangle
in rows of
Z\in\mathrm{Mat}^{>}_{1234567\times[k+4]}
using the relation
Z^{\prime}=[x_{1}(s)]\cdot[y_{7}(t)]\cdot[y_{6}(t^{\prime})]\cdot[y_{5}(t^{\prime\prime})]\cdot Z
, with coefficients depending on
t,t^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},s
. Lemma
4.36
allows us to express
t,t^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime},s
directly using
C,Z
-twistors.
By the first case of Lemma
4.36
, we obtain
Z^{\prime}_{3}=Z_{3}
,
Z^{\prime}_{4}=Z_{4}
, and
Z^{\prime}_{5}=Z_{5}
. The second case yields

\left\langle\widetilde{Z}(C)\,1\,2\,5\,6\right\rangle\,Z^{\prime}_{7}\;=\;\left\langle\widetilde{Z}(C)\,1\,2\,5\,6\right\rangle\,Z_{7}-\left\langle\widetilde{Z}(C)\,1\,2\,5\,7\right\rangle\,Z_{6}+\left\langle\widetilde{Z}(C)\,1\,2\,6\,7\right\rangle\,Z_{5}

Substituting these
Z^{\prime}_{j}
in
\langle\widetilde{Z}(C)\,Z^{\prime}_{3}\,Z^{\prime}_{4}\,Z^{\prime}_{5}\,Z^{\prime}_{7}\rangle
, we obtain

\left\langle\widetilde{Z}(C)\,1\,2\,5\,6\right\rangle\;\left\langle\widetilde{Z}(C)\,Z^{\prime}_{3}\,Z^{\prime}_{4}\,Z^{\prime}_{5}\,Z^{\prime}_{7}\right\rangle\;=\;\left\langle\widetilde{Z}(C)\,1\,2\,5\,6\right\rangle\;\left\langle\widetilde{Z}(C)\,3\,4\,5\,7\right\rangle-\left\langle\widetilde{Z}(C)\,1\,2\,5\,7\right\rangle\;\left\langle\widetilde{Z}(C)\,3\,4\,5\,6\right\rangle

Note that the third terms drops since
\langle 3\,4\,5\,5\rangle=0
, which demonstrates a case of Remark
4.30
. The twistor
\langle 1\,2\,5\,6\rangle
is nonnegative by Lemma
4.13
. If
C
is such that this twistor is strictly positive at
C
, then the above expression is nonzero. In conclusion, the promotion of
\langle 3\,4\,5\,7\rangle
under the upper embedding
\overleftrightarrow{\;\textsc{upp}\;}_{1}

is
\langle 1\,2\,5\,6\rangle\,\langle 3\,4\,5\,7\rangle-\langle 1\,2\,5\,7\rangle\,\langle 3\,4\,5\,6\rangle
, and it has the same fixed sign. By Definition
4.7
it is written also as
{\left\langle\!\left\langle\,6\,7\;{\big|}\;1\,2\;{\big|}\;3\,4\;{\big|}\;5\,\right\rangle\!\right\rangle}
, or as
{\left\langle\!\left\langle\,1\,2\;{\big|}\;3\,4\;{\big|}\;6\,7\;{\big|}\;5\,\right\rangle\!\right\rangle}
by Lemma
4.10
.

The following two propositions give a general version of the example above. We show under general conditions that upper and lower promotions transform arbitrary fixed-sign pure functionaries on a point to fixed-sign pure functionaries on the embedding of that point in a larger amplituhedron.

Proposition 4.42

(Fixed sign under upper promotion)
.

Let
F
be a pure functionary with indices in
N=\{i+1,\dots,n\}
. Let
F^{\prime}
be the upper promotion at
i
of
F
, with indices in
N\cup\{i\}
.
Let
C\in\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq}_{k-1,N}
and
t_{1},t_{2},t_{3},s_{1}>0,
and denote
C^{\prime}=\overleftrightarrow{\;\textsc{upp}\;}_{i}(t_{1},t_{2},t_{3},s_{1})C
.
Assume that

\langle\widetilde{Z}(C^{\prime})\;i\;i{+}1\;n{-}2\;n\rangle_{Z},\penalty\ \langle\widetilde{Z}(C^{\prime})\;i\;i{+}1\;n{-}2\;n{-}1\rangle_{Z}\;\neq\;0

for every
Z\in\mathrm{Mat}^{>}_{N\cup\{i\}\times[k+4]}
,
and
that
F(\langle\widetilde{Z}_{N}(C)\penalty\ Z_{I}\rangle)
has a fixed sign.
Then

\SIGN F^{\prime}(\langle\widetilde{Z}(C^{\prime})Z_{I}\rangle)\;=\;(-1)^{d_{n-1}(F)}\SIGN F(\langle\widetilde{Z}_{N}(C)\penalty\ Z_{I}\rangle)

Proof.

Let
Z\in\mathrm{Mat}^{>}_{N\cup\{i\}\times[k+4]}
be arbitrary and denote
Y=\widetilde{Z}(\overleftrightarrow{\;\textsc{upp}\;}_{i}(t_{1},t_{2},t_{3},s_{1})C)
.

Apply Lemma
4.28
with
N
as in this lemma and
l=3,r=1
, and note that
|I\cap[i]|=0
for any
I\in\binom{N}{4}
. We get that if
\SIGN F\left(\left\langle\widetilde{Z}_{N}\left(C\right)\penalty\ Z_{I}\right\rangle\right)=s\in\{\pm 1\}
then
\SIGN F^{\prime\prime}\left(\left\langle Y\penalty\ Z_{I}\right\rangle\right)=s,

where
F^{\prime\prime}\left(\left\langle Y\penalty\ Z_{I}\right\rangle\right)
is the functionary such that
F^{\prime\prime}\left(\left\langle Y\penalty\ Z_{I}\right\rangle\right)=F\left(\left\langle Y\penalty\ Z^{\prime}_{I}\right\rangle\right)
, and
Z^{\prime}
is the matrix described in that lemma.

We can find
F^{\prime\prime}
explicitly by linearly expanding the rows of
Z^{\prime}
as linear combinations of the rows of
Z
, expressing
F\left(\left\langle Y\penalty\ Z^{\prime}_{I}\right\rangle\right)
as another polynomial in
Z
-twistors, with some coefficients depending on
t_{i},s_{i}
. Lemma
4.36
allows us to express
t_{i},s_{i}
using twistors, and therefore express the rows of the matrix
Z^{\prime}
as linear combinations of rows of
Z
with coefficients which are rational functions of twistors.

Apply Lemma
4.36
with
l=3
, the same
N
, and
J=\{n-2,n-1,n,i,i+1\}
. For
j\notin\{n-1,n,i\}
we have
Z^{\prime}_{j}=Z_{j}
, and for
j=i
the vector
Z^{\prime}_{i}
does not appear in the functionary
F
. For the other possibilities for
j
,

\langle YZ_{\{n-2,n,i,i+1\}}\rangle Z^{\prime}_{n-1}=\langle YZ_{\{n-2,n,i,i+1\}}\rangle Z_{n-1}-\langle YZ_{\{n-1,n,i,i+1\}}\rangle Z_{n-2},

\langle YZ_{\{n-2,n-1,i,i+1\}}\rangle Z^{\prime}_{n}=\langle YZ_{\{n-2,n-1,i,i+1\}}\rangle Z_{n}-\langle YZ_{\{n-2,n,i,i+1\}}\rangle Z_{n-1}+\langle YZ_{\{n-1,n,i,i+1\}}\rangle Z^{\prime}_{n-2}.

After dividing by
\langle YZ_{\{n-2,n,i,i+1\}}\rangle
,
\langle YZ_{\{n-2,n-1,i,i+1\}}\rangle
respectively (using the assumption that they are nonzero), this is plugged into
F\left(\left\langle Y\penalty\ Z^{\prime}_{I}\right\rangle\right)
and expanded multilinearly, giving a rational function in twistors with the same fixed sign as
F^{\prime\prime}
, but independent of
t_{i},s_{i}
. This last step is precisely the substitution operation from Defintion
4.37
(i.e., steps 1,2,3 there), which we have seen in this form as the first operation in the proof of Proposition
4.39
(upper promotion part).

As mentioned, the resulting expression in
Z
-twistors would have sign
s
.
The next step in Definition
4.37
and the proof of Proposition
4.39

is multiplication by the common denominators, which are
\langle YZ_{\{n-2,n,i,i+1\}}\rangle^{d_{n-1}(F)}
and
\langle YZ_{\{n-2,n-1,i,i+1\}}\rangle^{d_{n}(F)}
. This would change the sign by
(-1)^{d_{n-1}(F)},
since on the amplituhedron
\langle YZ_{\{n-2,n-1,i,i+1\}}\rangle\geq 0
(by Lemma
4.13
) and thus
\langle YZ_{\{n-2,n-1,i,i+1\}}\rangle\leq 0
(by Lemma
4.32
), and since both are assumed to be nonzero. Together, this gives us the functionary
F^{\prime}
in
Y,Z
, with the sign

(-1)^{d_{n-1}(F)}s
.
∎

Proposition 4.43

(Fixed sign under lower promotion)
.

Let
N=\{1,\dots,i+1,n-1,n\}
for
i\leq n-4
. Let
F
be a pure functionary with indices in
N\setminus\{n-1\}
. Let
F^{\prime}
be the lower promotion of
F
, with indices in
N\setminus\{n-1\}\cup\{n-2\}
.
Let
C\in\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq}_{k-1,N}
and
t_{1},t_{2},s_{1},s_{2}>0,
and denote
C^{\prime}=\overleftrightarrow{\;\textsc{low}\;}_{n-2}(t_{1},t_{2},s_{1},s_{2})C
. Assume that

\langle\widetilde{Z}(C^{\prime})\;i\;i{+}1\;n{-}1\;n\rangle_{Z},\penalty\ \langle\widetilde{Z}(C^{\prime})\;i\;n{-}2\;n{-}1\;n\rangle_{Z}\;\neq\;0

for every
Z\in\mathrm{Mat}^{>}_{N\cup\{n-2\}\times[k+4]}
, and that
F(\langle\widetilde{Z}_{N}(C)\penalty\ Z_{I}\rangle)
has a fixed sign.
Then

\SIGN F^{\prime}(\langle\widetilde{Z}(C^{\prime})\;Z_{I}\rangle)\;=\;(-1)^{d_{i{+}1}(F)+d_{n}(F)}\SIGN F(\langle\widetilde{Z}_{N}(C)\penalty\ Z_{I}\rangle).

Proof.

Let
Z\in\mathrm{Mat}^{>}_{(N\cup\{n-2\})\times[k+4]}
be arbitrary and denote
Y=\widetilde{Z}(\overleftrightarrow{\;\textsc{low}\;}_{i}(t_{1},t_{2},s_{1},s_{2})C)
.

Apply Lemma
4.28
with
N
as in this lemma and
l=r=2
. We get that if

\SIGN F\left(\left\langle\widetilde{Z}_{N}\left(C\right)\penalty\ Z_{I}\right\rangle\right)=s\in\{\pm 1\}

then

\SIGN F\left((-1)^{|I\cap[n-2]|}\left\langle Y\penalty\ Z^{\prime}_{I}\right\rangle\right)=s,

where
Z^{\prime}
is the matrix described in that lemma and
F\left((-1)^{|I\cap[n-2]|}\left\langle Y\penalty\ Z^{\prime}_{I}\right\rangle\right)
denotes by abuse of notation the functionary
F^{\prime\prime}(\langle Y\penalty\ Z_{I}\rangle)
satisfying everywhere
F^{\prime\prime}(\langle Y\penalty\ Z_{I}\rangle)=F\left((-1)^{|I\cap[n-2]|}\left\langle Y\penalty\ Z^{\prime}_{I}\right\rangle\right)
from Lemma
4.28
. Note that, since
Z_{n-1}
does not appear in the expression for
F,
for all relevant
4-
tuples
I,

|I\cap[n-2]|
is odd precisely when
n\in I.

Thus, using a similar abuse of notation,

\SIGN F\left(\left\langle Y\penalty\ Z^{\prime}_{I}\right\rangle\right)=(-1)^{d_{n}(F)}s.

To express
F^{\prime\prime}
explicitly, we want to linearly expand the rows of
Z^{\prime}
,
F\left(\left\langle Y\penalty\ Z^{\prime}_{I}\right\rangle\right)
using the expression of the rows of
Z^{\prime}
are linear combinations of the rows of
Z
. This would give us an expression with coefficients depending on
t_{i},s_{i}
. Lemma
4.36
will allow us to express these coefficients with twistors, giving us a rational function in
C,Z
-twistors which is equal everywhere to
F\left(\left\langle Y\penalty\ Z^{\prime}_{I}\right\rangle\right)

With this in mind, let us apply Lemma
4.36
with
l=2
, the same
N
, and
J=\{i,i+1,n-2,n-1,n\}
. For
j\notin\{i+1,n-2,n-1\}
we have
Z^{\prime}_{j}=Z_{j}
, and for
j=n-2,n-1
we assumed that the vectors
Z^{\prime}_{n-2},Z^{\prime}_{n-1}
do not appear in the functionary
F
. The remaining possibility is
j=i+1
, where

\langle YZ_{\{i,n-2,n-1,n\}}\rangle Z^{\prime}_{i+1}=\langle YZ_{\{i,n-2,n-1,n\}}\rangle Z_{i+1}-\langle YZ_{\{i+1,n-2,n-1,n\}}\rangle Z_{i}.

After dividing by the non-zero
\langle YZ_{\{i,n-2,n-1,n\}}\rangle
, this is plugged into
F\left(\left\langle Y\penalty\ Z^{\prime}_{I}\right\rangle\right)
and expanded multilinearly, giving a rational function in twistors with the same fixed sign
s
as
F^{\prime\prime}
, but independent of
t_{i},s_{i}
. This procedure is precisely the substitution operation for the lower promotion from
Definition
4.37
(steps 1,2,3 there), which we have seen in this form in as the first operation in the proof of the lower promotion part of Proposition
4.39
.
The resulting expression in
Z
-twistors would have sign
(-1)^{d_{n}(F)}s
.

The next steps in the proof of the lower promotion part of Proposition
4.39
is multiplication by the common denominator
\langle YZ_{\{i,n-2,n-1,n\}}\rangle^{d_{i+1}(F)}
. This twistor is assumed to be non-zero. It is in fact negative, since by Lemma

4.32
,
-\langle YZ_{\{i,n-2,n-1,n\}}\rangle/\langle YZ_{\{i,i+1,n-1,n\}}\rangle>0,
and the denominator is positive by Lemma
4.13
and the assumption.
This gives us the functionary
F^{\prime}
in
Y,Z
, with the sign
(-1)^{d_{i+1}(F)+d_{n}(F)}s
. Since the choice of
Z
was arbitrary, the desired fixed sign follows.
∎

In the following two corollaries, we give special cases of Propositions
4.42
and
4.43

for certain important functionaries, that are used later in the paper. These lemmas provides us with a sequence of functionaries that have a fixed sign on the whole images of
\overleftrightarrow{\;\textsc{upp}\;}_{i}
and
\overleftrightarrow{\;\textsc{low}\;}
.

Corollary 4.44
.

Let
i<j<n-3
, let
C\in\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq}_{k-1,\{i+1,\dots,n\}}
and for some positive
t_{1},t_{2},t_{3},s_{1}
let
C^{\prime}=\overleftrightarrow{\;\textsc{upp}\;}_{i}(t_{1},t_{2},t_{3},s_{1})\;C\in\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq}_{k,\{i,\dots,n\}}
.
Assume that
C
and
C^{\prime}
are such that
\langle\widetilde{Z}(C)\penalty\ j\;j+1\;n-1\;n\rangle_{Z}\neq 0
for all
Z
, and
\langle\widetilde{Z}(C^{\prime})\penalty\ i\;i{+}1\;n{-}1\;n\rangle_{Z}\neq 0
for all
Z
. Then

\SIGN{\left\langle\!\left\langle\,i\penalty\ i{+}1\;{\big|}\;j\penalty\ j{+}1\;{\big|}\;n{-}2\penalty\ n{-}1\;{\big|}\;n\,\right\rangle\!\right\rangle}_{\widetilde{Z}(C^{\prime}),Z}\;=\;-1

Proof.

For the sake of simplicity, we omit
Y
and
Z
from all twistor and functionary notations. The twistor
F=\langle j\;j+1\;n-1\;n\rangle
has a positive fixed sign at
C
by Lemma
4.13
and the nonzero assumption. We can apply Proposition
4.42
on
F
, since we assume that
\langle\widetilde{Z}(C^{\prime})\penalty\ i\;i{+}1\;n{-}1\;n\rangle_{Z}\neq 0
, and thus
\langle\widetilde{Z}(C^{\prime})\penalty\ i\;i{+}1\;n{-}2\;n{-}1\rangle_{Z}
is also nonzero by Lemma
4.32
. From the proposition we get that
F^{\prime}
, the upper promotion of
F
, is a negative fixed-sign functionary. From Proposition
4.39
and Lemma
4.10
,

\displaystyle F^{\prime}

\displaystyle\;=\;\langle i\,i{+}1\,n{-}2\,n{-}1\rangle\left(\langle j\,j{+}1\,n{-}1\,n\rangle\;\langle i\,i{+}1\,n{-}2\,n\rangle-\langle j\,j{+}1\,n-2\,n\rangle\;\langle i\,i{+}1\,n{-}1\,n\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\;=\;\langle i\,i{+}1\,n{-}2\,n{-}1\rangle\;{\left\langle\!\left\langle\,n{-}2\penalty\ n{-}1\;{\big|}\;i\penalty\ i{+}1\;{\big|}\;j\penalty\ j{+}1\;{\big|}\;n\,\right\rangle\!\right\rangle}

\displaystyle\;=\;\langle i\,i{+}1\,n{-}2\,n{-}1\rangle\;{\left\langle\!\left\langle\,i\penalty\ i{+}1\;{\big|}\;j\penalty\ j{+}1\;{\big|}\;n{-}2\penalty\ n{-}1\;{\big|}\;n\,\right\rangle\!\right\rangle},

and since we also assume that
\langle i\,i{+}1\,n{-}2\,n{-}1\rangle
is nonzero at
C^{\prime}
, and it is nonnegative by Lemma
4.13
, then
{\left\langle\!\left\langle\,i\penalty\ i{+}1\;{\big|}\;j\penalty\ j{+}1\;{\big|}\;n{-}2\penalty\ n{-}1\;{\big|}\;n\,\right\rangle\!\right\rangle}
is a indeed a negative fixed-sign functionary.
All inequalities hold independently of the choice of
Z
.
∎

Corollary 4.45
.

Let
j<i<n-3
, let
C\in\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq}_{k-1,N}
where
N=\{1,\dots,i+1,n-1,n\}
, and for some positive
t_{1},t_{2},s_{1},s_{2}
let
C^{\prime}=\overleftrightarrow{\;\textsc{low}\;}_{n-2}(t_{1},t_{2},s_{1},s_{2})\;C\in\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq}_{k,N\cup\{n-2\}}
.
Assume that
C
and
C^{\prime}
are such that
\SIGN\langle\widetilde{Z}(C)\;j\;j{+}1\;i+1\;n\rangle_{Z}=s\in\{\pm 1\}
and
\langle\widetilde{Z}(C^{\prime})\penalty\ i{+}1\;n{-}2\;n{-}1\;n\rangle_{Z}\neq 0
for all
Z
.
Then

\SIGN\,{\left\langle\!\left\langle\,j\penalty\ j{+}1\;{\big|}\;i\penalty\ i{+}1\;{\big|}\;n{-}2\penalty\ n{-}1\;{\big|}\;n\,\right\rangle\!\right\rangle}_{\widetilde{Z}(C^{\prime}),Z}\;=\;s

In particular, if
C={\mathrm{pre}}_{n-1}C^{\prime\prime}
for some
C^{\prime\prime}\;\in\;\mathrm{Gr}_{k-1,N\setminus\{n-1\}}^{\geq}
then
s=+1
.

Proof.

Denote by
F=\langle\widetilde{Z}_{N}(C)\;j\;j{+}1\;i+1\;n\rangle
the assumed fixed-sign functionary. We apply Proposition
4.43
where the twistors
\langle i\;i+1\;n-1\;n\rangle
and
\langle i\;n-2\;n-1\;n\rangle
are proportional to
\langle i{+}1\;n{-}2\;n{-}1\;n\rangle\neq 0
by Lemma
4.32
.
From Proposition
4.43
we get that
F^{\prime}
, the lower promotion of
F
, is a fixed-sign functionary with the same sign
s
.
From Proposition
4.39
and Lemma
4.10
,

\displaystyle F^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\langle j\penalty\ j{+}1\penalty\ i{+}1\penalty\ n\rangle\;\langle i\penalty\ n{-}2\penalty\ n{-}1\penalty\ n\rangle-\langle j\penalty\ j{+}1\penalty\ i\penalty\ n\rangle\;\langle i{+}1\penalty\ n{-}2\penalty\ n{-}1\penalty\ n\rangle

\displaystyle\;=\;{\left\langle\!\left\langle\,i\penalty\ i+1\;{\big|}\;n{-}2\penalty\ n{-}1\;{\big|}\;j\penalty\ j{+}1\;{\big|}\;n\,\right\rangle\!\right\rangle}

\displaystyle\;=\;{\left\langle\!\left\langle\,j\penalty\ j{+}1\;{\big|}\;i\penalty\ i+1\;{\big|}\;n{-}2\penalty\ n{-}1\;{\big|}\;n\,\right\rangle\!\right\rangle}

as the corollary claims.
∎

We conclude the section by working out explicitly two degenerate cases with
k=1
of Corollaries
4.44
and
4.45
. These examples demonstrate and verify that the fixed-sign functionaries obtained in the corollaries are correct in this end case as well.

Example 4.46

(following Corollary
4.44
)
.

We focus on the special case
i=1,\,k=1
of the previous proposition, where the promotion is applied on a
0\times(n-1)
matrix. After
\overleftrightarrow{\;\textsc{upp}\;}_{1}

the resulting
1\times n
matrix
C
has the form
(a_{1},a_{2},0,\ldots,0,a_{n-2},a_{n-1},a_{n})
, which is the domino matrix of one long top chord
(1,2,n-2,n-1)
. The following calculation shows that the quadratic functionary
{\left\langle\!\left\langle\,1\penalty\ 2\;{\big|}\;j\penalty\ j{+}1\;{\big|}\;n{-}2\penalty\ n{-}1\;{\big|}\;n\,\right\rangle\!\right\rangle}
is strictly negative, even in the end case
j=2
where some terms vanish.

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\langle 1\penalty\ j\penalty\ j{+}1\penalty\ n\rangle\,\langle 2\penalty\ n{-}2\penalty\ n{-}1\penalty\ n\rangle\,-\,\langle 2\penalty\ j\penalty\ j{+}1\penalty\ n\rangle\,\langle 1\penalty\ n{-}2\penalty\ n{-}1\penalty\ n\rangle

\displaystyle\;=\;\big(-a_{2}\langle Z_{1,2,j,j+1,n}\rangle-a_{n-2}\langle Z_{1,j,j+1,n-2,n}\rangle-a_{n-1}\langle Z_{1,j,j+1,n-1,n}\rangle\big)\cdot\big(+a_{1}\langle Z_{1,2,n-2,n-1,n}\rangle\big)

\displaystyle\;\;\;\;\;\;-\;\big(+a_{1}\langle Z_{1,2,j,j+1,n}\rangle-a_{n-2}\langle Z_{2,j,j+1,n-2,n}\rangle-a_{n-1}\langle Z_{2,j,j+1,n-1,n}\rangle\big)\cdot\big(-a_{2}\langle Z_{1,2,n-2,n-1,n}\rangle\big)

\displaystyle\;=\;\langle Z_{1,2,n-2,n-1,n}\rangle\,\big(-a_{1}a_{n-2}\langle Z_{1,j,j+1,n-2,n}\rangle-a_{1}a_{n-1}\langle Z_{1,j,j+1,n-1,n}\rangle

\displaystyle\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;-a_{2}a_{n-2}\langle Z_{2,j,j+1,n-2,n}\rangle-a_{2}a_{n-1}\langle Z_{2,j,j+1,n-1,n}\rangle\big),

where the terms
\pm a_{1}a_{2}\langle Z_{1,2,n-2,n-1,n}\rangle\;\langle Z_{1,2,j,j+1,n}\rangle
are cancelled out in the second equality.

Example 4.47

(following Corollary
4.45
)
.

We focus on the special case
i=n-4,\,k=1
of the previous proposition, where the promotion is applied on a
0\times(n-1)
matrix. We demonstrate with direct calculations that there is no exception in this case. After
\overleftrightarrow{\;\textsc{low}\;}_{n-2}

the resulting
1\times n
matrix
C
has the form
(0,\ldots,0,a_{n-4},a_{n{-}3},a_{n-2},a_{n-1},a_{n})
, which is the domino matrix of a single chord from
(n-4,n{-}3)
to
(n-2,n-1)
. For
j\leq n-5
, we expand in the first row every twistor that participates in the functionary
{\left\langle\!\left\langle\,j\penalty\ j{+}1\;{\big|}\;n{-}4\penalty\ n{-}3\;{\big|}\;n{-}2\penalty\ n{-}1\;{\big|}\;n\,\right\rangle\!\right\rangle}
.

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\langle j\penalty\ n{-}4\penalty\ n{-}3\penalty\ n\rangle\,\langle j{+}1\penalty\ n{-}2\penalty\ n{-}1\penalty\ n\rangle\,-\,\langle j{+}1\penalty\ n{-}4\penalty\ n{-}3\penalty\ n\rangle\,\langle j\penalty\ n{-}2\penalty\ n{-}1\penalty\ n\rangle

\displaystyle\;=\;(-a_{n-2}\langle Z_{j,n-4,n-3,n-2,n}\rangle-a_{n-1}\langle Z_{j,n-4,n-3,n-1,n}\rangle)

\displaystyle\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\cdot(-a_{n-4}\langle Z_{j+1,n-4,n-2,n-1,n}\rangle-a_{n-3}\langle Z_{j+1,n-3,n-2,n-1,n}\rangle)

\displaystyle\;-\;(-a_{n-2}\langle Z_{j+1,n-4,n-3,n-2,n}\rangle-a_{n-1}\langle Z_{j+1,n-4,n-3,n-1,n}\rangle)

\displaystyle\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\cdot(-a_{n-4}\langle Z_{j,n-4,n-2,n-1,n}\rangle-a_{n-3}\langle Z_{j,n-3,n-2,n-1,n}\rangle)

Expanding and regrouping,

\displaystyle\;=\;a_{n-2}a_{n-4}(\langle Z_{j,n-4,n-3,n-2,n}\rangle\;\langle Z_{j+1,n-4,n-2,n-1,n}\rangle-\langle Z_{j+1,n-4,n-3,n-2,n}\rangle\;\langle Z_{j,n-4,n-2,n-1,n}\rangle)

\displaystyle\;+\;a_{n-2}a_{n-3}(\langle Z_{j,n-4,n-3,n-2,n}\rangle\;\langle Z_{j+1,n-3,n-2,n-1,n}\rangle-\langle Z_{j+1,n-4,n-3,n-2,n}\rangle\;\langle Z_{j,n-3,n-2,n-1,n}\rangle)

\displaystyle\;+\;a_{n-1}a_{n-4}(\langle Z_{j,n-4,n-3,n-1,n}\langle Z_{j+1,n-4,n-2,n-1,n}\rangle-\langle Z_{j+1,n-4,n-3,n-1,n}\rangle\;\langle Z_{j,n-4,n-2,n-1,n}\rangle)

\displaystyle\;+\;a_{n-1}a_{n-3}(\langle Z_{j,n-4,n-3,n-1,n}\rangle\;\langle Z_{j+1,n-3,n-2,n-1,n}\rangle-\langle Z_{j+1,n-4,n-3,n-1,n}\rangle\;\langle Z_{j,n-3,n-2,n-1,n}\rangle)

Simplifying with the Plücker relations,

\displaystyle\;=\;

\displaystyle a_{n-2}a_{n-4}\langle Z_{j,j+1,n-4,n-2,n}\rangle\;\langle Z_{n-4,n-3,n-2,n-1,n}\rangle\;+\;a_{n-2}a_{n-3}\langle Z_{j,j+1,n-3,n-2,n}\rangle\;\langle Z_{n-4,n-3,n-2,n-1,n}\rangle

\displaystyle\;+\;

\displaystyle a_{n-1}a_{n-4}\langle Z_{j,j+1,n-4,n-1,n}\rangle\;\langle Z_{n-4,n-3,n-2,n-1,n}\rangle\;+\;a_{n-1}a_{n-3}\langle Z_{j,j+1,n-3,n-1,n}\rangle\;\langle Z_{n-4,n-3,n-2,n-1,n}\rangle

This expression is positive as required, even in the end case
j=n-5
, where some but not all of the terms vanish.

5
Injectivity

In this section we prove Theorem
1.3
, that each BCFW cell maps injectively onto its image by solving an inverse problem (Proposition
5.1
): given a point in the image of a BCFW cell
S
, we find its unique preimage in that cell.
By Theorem
3.46
there is a chord diagram
D
with chords
\{c_{i}\}_{i\in[k]}
such that every point in
S
has a representative matrix
C
, such that nonzero entries of every row
C_{i}
are precisely in the five or six positions
\Supp(c_{i})
, and with the right signs.
We can now
calculate the nonzero entries of
C
iteratively from
Z
and
Y=\widetilde{Z}(C)
.
We start with the top chords, and after calculating the entries for a row, we calculate the entries of each of its children.
We use Lemma
4.35
with
Y
and
Z_{j},Z_{j+1},Z_{l},Z_{l+1},v
where either
v=Z_{n}
or
v=t_{1}Z_{h}+t_{2}Z_{h+1}
.
We elaborate on this method in the proof of Proposition
5.1
. In Proposition
5.6
we generalize this beyond the nonnegative Grassmannian. Remarkably, the resulting preimage can be expressed using a functionary-valued matrix, see Definition
5.4
and Example
5.5
.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
S\in\mathcal{BCFW}_{n,k}
,
Z\in\mathrm{Mat}^{>}_{n\times(k+4)}
, and
Y\in\widetilde{Z}(S)
. Then,
Y
has a unique preimage under
\widetilde{Z}
. In other words, the restriction of
\widetilde{Z}
to
S
is injective.

We use the following lemma to prove the proposition.

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
S\in\mathcal{BCFW}_{n,k}
,
Z\in\mathrm{Mat}^{>}_{n\times(k+4)}
, and
Y\in\widetilde{Z}(S)
. For every chord
c_{i}=(j,j+1,l,l+1)
in the chord diagram of
S
:

1.

If
c_{i}
is a top chord then
\langle YZ_{I}\rangle\neq 0
for every
I\subseteq\{j,j+1,l,l+1,n\}
of size
|I|=4
.

2.

If
c_{i}
is a child of another chord then
\langle YZ_{\{j,j+1,l,l+1\}}\rangle\neq 0
.

Proof of Proposition
5.1
.

By Definition
2.28
,
S
comes from some chord diagram
D
, and by Theorem
3.46
every point in
S
must have a unique representation in the domino form of
D
, up to rescaling of rows. Therefore, for any
Y\in\widetilde{Z}(S)
there is some preimage
C\in S
, which we can uniquely represent as a domino matrix in the form
D
.
On the other hand, any preimage of
Y
in
S
can be represented in the domino form
D
. Therefore, to show the uniqueness of the preimage, we must show that the row entries of a domino matrix
C
such that
CZ
has the same row span as
Y
are uniquely determined by
Y
and
Z
. We now show this to each row
c_{i}
of
C
.

If
c_{i}=(j,j+1,l,l+1)
is a top level chord
then on the one hand
Y_{i}
belongs to the row span of
Y,
and on the other hand it is a linear combination of
Z_{s}
for
s\in\{j,j+1,l,l+1,n\}.

The five vectors
Z_{s}
for
s\in\{j,j+1,l,l+1,n\}
are linearly independent, since
k\geq 1
and
Z
is a positive
n\times(k+4)
matrix, so every collection of different
k+4
rows is linearly independent.
By Lemma
4.35
and the first case of Lemma
5.2
,
C_{i}
is uniquely determined, up to rescaling, as the correct
i
th row of
C
.

Suppose now that
c_{i}
is not a top chord,
but we have found the unique possible representative for the row of its parent
C_{p}
. In this case
k\geq 2.

Since
C
is a domino matrix and
C_{p}
is its
p
th row, the entries
t_{1}=\alpha_{p}
and
t_{2}=\beta_{p}
are both nonzero.
Therefore if
(h,h+1)
is the start of
c_{p}
then
v=t_{1}Z_{h}+t_{2}Z_{h+1}\neq 0.

As before,
Y_{i}
is both in the row span of
Y
and in the linear span of
\{v,Z_{j},Z_{j+1},Z_{l},Z_{l+1}\}
. These five vectors are again linearly independent. When
h+1=j
this follows from the linear independence of the five vectors
\{Z_{h},Z_{h+1},Z_{j+1},Z_{l},Z_{l+1}\}
, which in turn follows from the positivity of
Z
. Otherwise,
h+1<j
and it follows from the independence of the six vectors
\{Z_{h},Z_{h+1},Z_{j},Z_{j+1},Z_{l},Z_{l+1}\}
, because
Z
is positive and
k\geq 2.
Hence, by Lemma
4.35
and the second case of Lemma
5.2
the entries of
C_{i}
are uniquely recovered, up to rescaling, and we obtain the correct row in the domino matrix
C
. Repeating for every chord, we recover the whole matrix
C
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
5.2
.

We start with the first case. Suppose that
Y=CZ,
where
C
is the
domino form of an arbitrary point
V\in S
. In this case,
C_{i}
has support
A=\{j,j+1,l,l+1,n\}.
Let
I=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{4}\}\subset A
be as in the statement, and
\{p\}=A\setminus I.

Then by Lemma
4.11
and the fact that
C_{i}
is supported on the entries of
A,

\langle YZ_{I}\rangle=\sum_{{J\in\binom{[n]\setminus I}{k}},\penalty\ p\in J}\langle C^{J}\rangle\langle Z_{J\cup I}\rangle s(J,I).

If
\langle C^{J}\rangle\neq 0
for a given
J,
then there must be a bijection
f:[k]\to J
such that
C_{h}^{f(h)}\neq 0,
or equivalently the entry
f(h)
is in the support of
C_{h}.
Since
c_{i}
is a top chord, the supports of the rows that correspond to chords that start before
c_{i}
are contained in
[j+1]\cup\{n\}.
The supports of rows for chords that descend from
c_{i}
are contained in
[l+1]\setminus[j+1].
The support of the remaining rows (other than
C_{i}
) are contained in
[n]\setminus[l].

Thus, if
\langle C^{J}\rangle\neq 0
, then the conditions
{J\in\binom{[n]\setminus I}{k}},\penalty\ p\in J
and the fact that
c_{i}
is a top chord imply:

•

k_{1}=|J\cap[j-1]|
is the number of chords that start before
c_{i}
, which is
i-1
.

•

k_{2}=|J\cap\{j+2,\ldots,l-1\}|
is the number of chords that descend from
c_{i}
, which is
\mathrm{below}(c_{i})

•

k_{3}=|J\cap\{l+2,\ldots,n-1\}|
is the number of chords that start after
c_{i}
ends:
\mathrm{behind}(c_{i})-\mathrm{below}(c_{i}).

Hence, for every
J
in the above sum that contributes a nonzero determinant, the sign
s(J,A\setminus\{p\})
as defined in Lemma
4.11
depends solely on
p
, as follows.

s(J,A\setminus\{p\})=\begin{cases}(-1)^{k_{2}+k_{3}}=(-1)^{\mathrm{behind}(c_{i})},\penalty\ \text{if }p=j,\\
(-1)^{1+k_{2}+k_{3}}=(-1)^{\mathrm{behind}(c_{i})+1},\penalty\ \text{if }p=j+1,\\
(-1)^{k_{3}}=(-1)^{\mathrm{behind}(c_{i})-\mathrm{below}(c_{i})},\penalty\ \text{if }p=l,\\
(-1)^{1+k_{3}}=(-1)^{\mathrm{behind}(c_{i})-\mathrm{below}(c_{i})+1},\penalty\ \text{if }p=l+1,\\
1,\penalty\ \text{if }p=n.\end{cases}

Thus, it is enough to show that there is a set
J\in\binom{[n]}{k}

with
J\cap\{j,j+1,l,l+1,n\}=\{p\}
and
\langle C^{J}\rangle\neq 0,
but this follows from Lemma
3.32
.

We turn to the second case.
Write
c=c_{i}=(j,j+1,l,l+1).
We show that
\langle Yj,j+1,l,l+1\rangle
is non vanishing for all
Z
and
Y\in\widetilde{Z}(S)
. The strategy is to start with the subdiagram
D^{\prime}
that descends from
c
, that is, made of the chord
c
and its descendants, erasing the markers outside, except for
n
. Then one uses Corollaries
3.47
-
3.49
to extend it to the full diagram. We show how the twistor of interest evolves during this process, and that it remains nonzero, for all positive
Z,
and even keeps its sign. We note that a direct proof for this claim can be produced along the lines of Lemma
3.32
, but the proof we now show is simpler, and demonstrates the promotion technique, which is crucial in Section
6
below, where direct proofs seem less accessible.

Let
D^{\prime}
be the subdiagram which descends from
c
and
S^{\prime}
the corresponding positroid cell. In
D^{\prime}
,
c
is a top chord, hence Lemma
5.2
,(
1
) applies. Thus, for all positive matrices
Z^{\prime}
of the appropriate size, on
\widetilde{Z}^{\prime}(S^{\prime})
it holds that

\langle j,j+1,l,l+1\rangle\;>\;0

By Corollaries
3.47
-
3.49
,
S
is obtained from
S^{\prime}
by a series of operations of the form:

1.

{\mathrm{inc}}_{h},{\mathrm{pre}}_{h}
for
h\notin\{j,j+1,\ldots,l+1\}\cup\{n\}.

2.

x_{h}(t),\penalty\ t\in\mathbb{R}_{+},
for
h\notin\{j+1,\ldots,l+1\}\cup\{n\},
or
y_{h}(t),\penalty\ t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
for
h\notin\{j-1,\ldots,l-1\}\cup\{n{{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}_{N^{\prime}}1}\},
where
{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}
is calculated with respect to the index set at the time of the application of
y_{h}(t).

3.

Upper embeddings with respect to a chord which starts before
(j,j+1)
and ends at
(l,l+1)
or after. It has the form
\overleftrightarrow{\;\textsc{upp}\;}_{i}(t_{1},t_{2},t_{3},s_{1}),\penalty\ t_{1},t_{2},t_{3},s_{1}\in\mathbb{R}_{+},
where
i{{\pluscirc}1}\leq j
and
i{{\,\;\mathclap{\circ}\mathclap{\relbar}\;\,}3}\geq l.

All these operations preserve the form and the sign of the twistor
\langle j,j+1,l,l+1\rangle.
Indeed, for
{\mathrm{pre}}_{h},{\mathrm{inc}}_{h}
this follows from Lemmas
4.20
and
4.22
, where for
{\mathrm{inc}}_{h}
we use that
|I\cap[h]|
is either
0
or
4
for each twistor which participates in the expressions for the determinants, by the restrictions on
h.

For
x_{h},y_{h}
this follows from Lemma
4.26
and the restrictions on
h.

For upper embeddings it follows from Remark
4.30
and the constraints on
h
. Thus, each step keeps this twistor positive, for all
Z
.
∎

Remark 5.3
.

The method from the proof of Proposition
5.1
for calculating the entries of a row that is supported on
m+1
entries using twistor coordinates has previously appeared in the literature. The appendix of
[
AM23
, Appendix A]
provides one example for
m=4.
Another example for
m=2
appears in
[
PSW21
, Section 4]
. The novelty of the above procedure is that it allows, in the context of BCFW cells, to calculate entries in rows of larger support, which is generally the case for descendants.

Note that we can take the coefficients we get from applying Lemma
4.35
in the proof of Proposition
5.1
to be simply the twistors from the statement of the lemma, and
t_{1},t_{2}
can be expressed also as functionaries. This enables the following definition.

Definition 5.4
.

For
S\in\mathcal{BCFW}_{n,k}
let
D
be the corresponding chord diagram. The unique preimage
C
of an arbitrary
Y\in\widetilde{Z}(S)
can be expressed as a functionary-valued matrix which is a
D
-domino matrix. Denote this representation
C_{D}(Y,Z)
.

Example 5.5
.

Consider the cell of
D=([8],((1,2,6,7),(2,3,6,7),(4,5,6,7))\in\mathcal{CD}_{8,3}
. It has the following domino form:

\begin{array}[]{*{8}{c}}\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}1&\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}2&\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}3&\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}4&\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}5&\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}6&\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}7&\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}8\\
\hline\cr\alpha_{1}&\beta_{1}&0&0&0&\gamma_{1}&\delta_{1}&\varepsilon_{1}\\
\!\varepsilon_{2}\alpha_{1}&\!\!\!\varepsilon_{2}\beta_{1}{+}\alpha_{2}&\beta_{2}&0&0&\gamma_{2}&\delta_{2}&0\\
0&\varepsilon_{3}\alpha_{2}&\varepsilon_{3}\beta_{2}&\!\alpha_{3}&\!\beta_{3}&\gamma_{3}&\delta_{3}&0\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\vskip 5.0pt

We derive the functionary-valued matrix
C_{D}(Y,Z)
. As in the proof of Proposition
5.1
, we fill the domino matrix with functionaries, row by row. For a top chord, such as Row 1, the domino variables
\alpha_{i},\beta_{i},\gamma_{i},\delta_{i},\varepsilon_{i}
are the five twistors obtained using Lemma
4.35
. For a child chord, such as Row 3, we use the known
\alpha_{p},\beta_{p}
of the parent to reduce the problem to five effective entries, and recover
\alpha_{i},\beta_{i},\gamma_{i},\delta_{i},\varepsilon_{i}
from the lemma. It follows that
\varepsilon_{i}
is a twistor, and the other four are combinations of two twistors with the functionary coefficients
\alpha_{p},\beta_{p}
. Functionaries for a sticky child such as Row 2 may be recovered as either of the two cases, and here we demonstrate the latter.

We use the notation
\langle abc|xy|def\rangle=\langle abcx\rangle\,\langle ydef\rangle-\langle abcy\rangle\,\langle xdef\rangle
for such quadratic functionaries, and

\langle abc|xy|de|zw|ghi\rangle=\langle abc|xy|dez\rangle\,\langle wghi\rangle-\langle abc|xy|dew\rangle\,\langle zghi\rangle
for cubic functionaries of this form. The resulting functionary-valued matrix is the following one, broken into two parts to fit in the page.

\begin{array}[]{*{8}{c}}\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}1&\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}2&\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}3&\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}4\\
\hline\cr\langle 2678\rangle&-\langle 1678\rangle&0&0\\
\langle 2367\rangle\langle 2678\rangle&-\langle 2367\rangle\langle 1678\rangle-\langle 678|12|367\rangle&\langle 678|12|267\rangle&0\\
0&-\langle 4567\rangle\langle 678|12|367\rangle&\langle 4567\rangle\langle 678|12|267\rangle&-\langle 678|12|67|23|567\rangle\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\hskip 54.06006pt\vskip 5.0pt

\hskip 136.5733pt\begin{array}[]{*{8}{c}}\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}5&\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}6&\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}7&\color[rgb]{0.75,0.75,0.75}8\\
\hline\cr 0&\langle 1278\rangle&-\langle 1268\rangle&\langle 1267\rangle\\
0&-\langle 678|12|237\rangle&\langle 678|12|236\rangle&0\\
\langle 678|12|67|23|467\rangle&-\langle 678|12|67|23|457\rangle&\langle 678|12|67|23|456\rangle&0\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\vskip 5.0pt

Note that it is possible to simplify the expression in the second row by rescaling by a factor of
\langle 2678\rangle^{-1}
, and obtain a row of five twistors as expected.

For an arbitrary
Y\in\mathrm{Gr}_{k,k+4}
the matrix
C_{D}(Y,Z)
is always a
D
-domino matrix, even though it might not be of full rank, or nonnegative. The following generalization of Proposition
5.1
will be useful in later sections. Recall the subspace
\widetilde{S_{\partial\mathcal{A}}}
from Definition
4.16
.

Proposition 5.6
.

Let
D
be a chord diagram and
Z
a positive matrix. Consider the subspace of the (whole) Grassmannian consisting of vector spaces that have a
D
-domino representative in the sense of Definition
2.5
, but do not necessarily satisfy the sign constraints of Definition
2.10
. Denote by
S^{\diamondsuit}=S^{\diamondsuit}_{D}
the subset of this space made of elements which do not belong to
\widetilde{S_{\partial\mathcal{A}}}
. Then the restriction of
\widetilde{Z}
to
S^{\diamondsuit}
is injective. The unique preimage of every
Y\in\widetilde{Z}(S^{\diamondsuit})
is precisely
C_{D}(Y,Z)
. In particular, the inverse map from
\widetilde{Z}(S^{\diamondsuit})
to
S^{\diamondsuit}
is smooth.

Proof.

First of all, the amplituhedron map is defined on
S^{\diamondsuit},
by the definition of
\widetilde{S_{\partial\mathcal{A}}}
.

The proof of the proposition is almost identical to the proof of Proposition
5.1
, differing only in the verification of the assumptions of Lemma
4.35
, and in that now it is given that the preimage of
Y
has a representation in the domino form of
D
(but now with weaker constraints defining
S^{\diamondsuit}
).
